1	**LoRA Fine-Tuning Strategy**
2	
3	**for Rune's Qwen3.5-9B Adapter Pipeline**
4	
5	Elixir Trials --- March 31, 2026 (Rev. 2)
6	
7	1\. Pipeline Overview
8	
9	Rune's adapter pipeline is a single sequential process with two stages.
10	Stage 1 (QLoRA bootstrapping) produces a corpus of high-quality adapters
11	via gradient descent. Stage 2 (hypernetwork training) trains a model to
12	approximate Stage 1's output in real time. The hypernetwork is the
13	production system; QLoRA is the mechanism that creates its training
14	data.
15	
16	The core objective is to produce adapters that encode *procedural
17	knowledge* from coding trajectories---not just factual recall. An
18	adapter generated from a trajectory of "generate → execute → error →
19	diagnose → fix" should enable the model to apply that repair pattern to
20	novel, structurally similar errors. This is distinct from RAG (which
21	consumes context tokens) and from full fine-tuning (which risks
22	catastrophic forgetting).
23	
24	  --------------------- ------------------------------------------------------- ---------------------------------------------------
25	                        **Stage 1: QLoRA Bootstrapping**                        **Stage 2: Hypernetwork Production**
26	  Purpose               Build high-quality adapter corpus                       Generate adapters in real time during agent loop
27	  Mechanism             Gradient descent on trajectory SFT data                 Single forward pass through DocToLoraHypernetwork
28	  Starting checkpoint   DeltaCoder adapter (warm-start)                         Sakana qwen\_4b\_d2l aggregator (transfer)
29	  Rank                  64 (inherited from DeltaCoder)                          8--32 (see Section 3.3)
30	  Latency               Minutes per adapter (GPU-bound)                         Milliseconds per adapter (CPU-capable)
31	  Quality               High (gradient-optimized)                               Approximate (amortized)
32	  Volume                50--100 adapters (bootstrapping)                        Unlimited (on-demand generation)
33	  When it runs          Offline corpus building + occasional high-value tasks   Real-time within the agent loop
34	  --------------------- ------------------------------------------------------- ---------------------------------------------------
35	
36	Once the hypernetwork is trained, QLoRA's ongoing role shrinks to
37	producing occasional adapters for tasks where the hypernetwork's
38	approximation is insufficient. Those adapters feed back into periodic
39	hypernetwork retraining.
40	
41	2\. Stage 1: QLoRA Fine-Tuning with DeltaCoder Warm-Start
42	
43	2.1 The DeltaCoder Warm-Start
44	
45	Rather than initializing new LoRA matrices from scratch, Stage 1 loads
46	**danielcherubini/Qwen3.5-DeltaCoder-9B** via
47	PeftModel.from\_pretrained, sets requires\_grad=True on the adapter
48	parameters, and continues training on Rune's trajectory data. This
49	provides three advantages:
50	
51	-   **GDN coverage inherited:** DeltaCoder already targets all 12 module
52	    types (5 GDN + 4 attention + 3 MLP). No manual module list
53	    configuration needed.
54	
55	-   **Convergence acceleration:** Starting from pretrained weights skips
56	    the initial convergence phase. DeltaCoder was trained on 50K
57	    CoderForge tool-call examples and DPO-aligned on self-correction
58	    pairs---directly relevant to Rune's trajectory domain.
59	
60	-   **DPO alignment preserved:** DeltaCoder's DPO stage on AceCode-V2
61	    preference pairs gives it self-correction behavior that maps to
62	    Rune's Phase 5 diagnose→repair loop.
63	
64	Prerequisite: Validate DeltaCoder before warm-starting. Load via
65	PeftModel.from\_pretrained, confirm no shape errors, run ≥50 HumanEval
66	samples. If it degrades the base model, fall back to fresh LoRA
67	initialization using DeltaCoder's target module list as the
68	configuration template.
69	
70	2.2 Training Hyperparameters
71	
72	  ------------------ ---------------------------------------------- -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
73	  **Parameter**      **Value**                                      **Rationale**
74	  LoRA rank          64 (inherited from DeltaCoder)                 Matching rank avoids weight\_head reshape; sufficient for code trajectories
75	  LoRA alpha         32 (alpha = rank/2)                            DeltaCoder convention; PEFT scale = 0.5 (conservative)
76	  Learning rate      2e-4                                           Constant schedule with warmup; cosine decay risks premature convergence on small datasets
77	  LR schedule        Constant + warmup (10 steps)                   Avoids oscillation on small trajectory datasets
78	  Loss computation   Assistant tokens only                          Mask system/user turns; compute loss only on model outputs
79	  Attention impl     eager (NOT flash\_attention\_2)                Flash + sample packing causes loss collapse on Qwen3.5
80	  Sequence length    4,096--65,536 tokens                           Depends on trajectory length distribution
81	  Precision          bf16                                           NF4 quantized base, bf16 adapter weights
82	  Batch size         16--32 effective (via gradient accumulation)   Per-device batch=1, grad\_accum=16--32
83	  Per-turn EOS       Yes                                            Train clean stop at turn boundaries
84	  Quantization       NF4 double-quantized                           Matches existing trainer.py; VRAM \~12--17GB
85	  Epochs             3                                              Standard for small trajectory datasets
86	  ------------------ ---------------------------------------------- -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
87	
88	2.3 GDN-Aware Target Modules
89	
90	The full target module list for Qwen3.5-9B, inherited from DeltaCoder:
91	
92	  ----------------------- ----------------------------------------------------------------- --------------------------------
93	  **Category**            **Modules**                                                       **Layers Affected**
94	  GDN projections         in\_proj\_qkv, in\_proj\_z, in\_proj\_b, in\_proj\_a, out\_proj   24/32 (indices where i%4 != 3)
95	  Attention projections   q\_proj, k\_proj, v\_proj, o\_proj                                8/32 (indices 3,7,11,\...,31)
96	  MLP projections         gate\_proj, up\_proj, down\_proj                                  All 32 layers
97	  ----------------------- ----------------------------------------------------------------- --------------------------------
98	
99	When warm-starting from DeltaCoder, this module list is already baked
100	into the adapter's config. When initializing fresh (fallback case), the
101	list must be set explicitly.
102	
103	2.4 Training Data Pipeline
104	
105	1.  **Collection:** The rune-agent loop (generate → execute → reflect)
106	    produces CodingSession trajectories stored as JSON via trajectory.py
107	
108	2.  **Formatting:** format\_for\_sft() converts trajectories to
109	    3-message chat format (system, user, assistant).
110	    format\_for\_distillation() handles the KL-divergence variant
111	
112	3.  **Augmentation:** augment\_trajectories() generates LLM-augmented
113	    variants via Ollama (3 variants per trajectory by default)
114	
115	4.  **Splitting:** split\_by\_task\_id() ensures train/test splits
116	    preserve task\_id integrity (no data leakage)
117	
118	Both successful and failed trajectories are training signals. Successful
119	trajectories encode working solution patterns. Exhausted trajectories
120	(max attempts reached) encode diagnose→repair patterns critical for
121	error recovery. The initial corpus should span at least 3 task types
122	(bug-fix, feature-impl, refactor).
123	
124	3\. Stage 2: Hypernetwork Training
125	
126	Once Stage 1 produces the adapter corpus, the hypernetwork is trained to
127	approximate QLoRA's output in a single forward pass. The hypernetwork is
128	the component that makes Rune's real-time adapter generation
129	possible---without it, every new trajectory would require minutes of
130	GPU-bound gradient descent before it could inform the model's behavior.
131	
132	3.1 Sakana Aggregator Transfer
133	
134	Training the hypernetwork from random initialization has been shown to
135	fail (farpluto/doc-to-lora-niah-qwen3.5-2B achieves 0% exact-match on
136	NIAH). The recommended path:
137	
138	1.  **Download** qwen\_4b\_d2l **checkpoint** from Sakana's
139	    SakanaAI/doc-to-lora repository. This variant is preferred over
140	    gemma\_demo or mistral\_7b\_d2l because its aggregator was trained
141	    on a Qwen-family model, making feature representations more
142	    transferable.
143	
144	2.  **Transfer aggregator weights** via transfer\_aggregator\_weights():
145	    loads aggregator.\* parameters, freezes them (requires\_grad=False).
146	
147	3.  **Retrain head.\* only** against the Stage 1 adapter corpus. This
148	    leverages Sakana's pretrained Perceiver feature representations
149	    while adapting the output projection to Qwen3.5-9B's architecture
150	    and Rune's trajectory domain.
151	
152	Note the distinction: DeltaCoder is the warm-start for *Stage 1* (the
153	QLoRA adapters). Sakana is the warm-start for *Stage 2* (the
154	hypernetwork itself). They operate on different components of the
155	system.
156	
157	3.2 KL-Divergence Context Distillation
158	
159	The training loop uses a two-pass strategy per step: (1) extract
160	activations from the base model given the trajectory in-context
161	(teacher), (2) generate adapter weights via the hypernetwork and apply
162	them to the base model with no trajectory in context (student), (3)
163	compute the blended loss:
164	
165	L = α · KL(student \|\| teacher) + (1 - α) · CE(student, labels)
166	
167	where α=0.5. The adapter must reconstruct the teacher's behavior from
168	weight-space alone---this is what makes it a *parametric* episodic
169	memory rather than a context-based one. An enhancement worth testing:
170	use base+DeltaCoder as the teacher instead of base alone, giving the
171	hypernetwork a stronger target distribution to approximate.
172	
173	3.3 Hypernetwork Rank: Why Rank-8, and When to Increase
174	
175	The current default rank of 8 comes from the Sakana qwen\_4b\_d2l
176	checkpoint, which was trained at that rank. Starting at rank-8 preserves
177	the transfer benefit. But rank-8 may be too conservative for
178	Qwen3.5-9B's 12-module GDN architecture, and the rank is the primary
179	knob controlling the expressiveness vs. cost tradeoff of
180	hypernetwork-generated adapters.
181	
182	The key constraint is the weight\_head output dimension, which scales
183	linearly with rank:
184	
185	  ----------------------- ----------------------------------- ----------------------------- -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
186	  **Hypernetwork Rank**   **weight\_head Output (approx.)**   **weight\_head Parameters**   **Practical Concern**
187	  8                       \~25M                               \~200M                        Fits current architecture; Sakana transfer applies directly
188	  16                      \~50M                               \~400M                        2× cost; likely the sweet spot for expressiveness vs. size
189	  32                      \~100M                              \~800M                        Approaching diminishing returns; consider chunked heads
190	  64                      \~200M                              \~1.6B                        weight\_head larger than some target models; not recommended for single linear projection
191	  ----------------------- ----------------------------------- ----------------------------- -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
192	
193	The Perceiver latent is 32 × 256 = 8,192 dimensions. Projecting from
194	8,192 to 200M (rank-64) via a single linear layer is a 1.6B-parameter
195	matrix---expensive to train and prone to overfitting on 50--100
196	trajectories. At rank-16 or above, the single weight\_head should be
197	replaced with a per-module or chunked head architecture. The
198	per\_rank\_gen flag in d2l\_config.py (currently set to False) provides
199	a starting point: it generates each rank dimension independently, which
200	distributes the projection across smaller matrices.
201	
202	**Recommendation:** Start at rank-8 to validate the pipeline end-to-end
203	with Sakana transfer. Once working, sweep rank ∈ {8, 16, 32} and measure
204	Pass\@1 vs. generation latency. If rank-16 closes \>80% of the gap to
205	QLoRA rank-64, adopt it. Enable per\_rank\_gen when rank exceeds 16.
206	
207	3.4 Training Parameters
208	
209	  ------------------- ------------------------------ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
210	  **Parameter**       **Value**                      **Source**
211	  Latent dimensions   32 latents × 256 dims          DocToLoraHypernetwork defaults
212	  Perceiver depth     4 layers, 8 heads              DocToLoraHypernetwork defaults
213	  Output rank         8 (initial; ablation target)   Sakana checkpoint + sweep plan
214	  Learning rate       2e-4                           D2LTrainConfig default
215	  KL blend α          0.5                            Equal weight to KL divergence and CE loss
216	  Temperature         2.0                            KL distillation temperature
217	  Gradient clipping   1.0                            D2LTrainConfig default
218	  Warmup steps        10                             D2LTrainConfig default
219	  Max input length    512 tokens                     Activation extraction window
220	  Scaling factor      0.16                           Bayesian-optimized (200 trials); Sakana default of 45.25× is far too aggressive
221	  ------------------- ------------------------------ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
222	
223	4\. Adapter Evolution and Lifecycle
224	
225	4.1 Fitness-Driven Selection
226	
227	Each adapter is scored using a weighted fitness function:
228	
229	φ(a) = 0.7 · pass\_rate(a) + 0.3 · generalization(a)
230	
231	where pass\_rate is measured on the training task and generalization is
232	measured on held-out tasks of the same type. The weight α=0.7 biases
233	toward specialization; it is an ablation target.
234	
235	4.2 Evolution Operations
236	
237	  --------------- ------------------------------------------------------------ ------------------------------------------------------------------------
238	  **Operation**   **Trigger**                                                  **Effect**
239	  Consolidate     ≥5 adapters for a task type, top-3 similarity \> threshold   TIES-merge top-3 into domain-level adapter
240	  Update          New adapter has higher fitness for same task type            Archive old adapter, register new one
241	  Forget          Adapter fitness \< 0.3                                       Soft delete (is\_archived = 1)
242	  Merge           Periodic sweep (evolution\_interval seconds)                 TIES/DARE merge top-3 per task type; new generation = max(parents) + 1
243	  --------------- ------------------------------------------------------------ ------------------------------------------------------------------------
244	
245	4.3 TIES vs DARE Merging
246	
247	**TIES** (density=0.5) prunes 50% of parameters and resolves sign
248	conflicts by majority vote---preferred when adapters are diverse
249	(consolidating across subtask types). **DARE** (drop\_rate=0.1) randomly
250	drops 10% and rescales---preferred when adapters are similar (averaging
251	multiple runs of the same task). Both are implemented in merging.py.
252	
253	4.4 Lineage Tracking
254	
255	The adapter registry stores parent\_ids (JSON list) and generation
256	(integer) for every adapter, enabling full lineage tracing. Write-once
257	enforcement ensures adapter weights and hashes are immutable---only
258	metadata (fitness, archived status) is mutable.
259	
260	5\. Validation and Kill-Switch Gate
261	
262	5.1 Primary Gate: Pass\@1 Improvement
263	
264	The kill-switch requires ≥5% Pass\@1 improvement on HumanEval over the
265	unmodified base model:
266	
267	-   **Benchmark:** HumanEval, 20--30 held-out tasks, k=5 samples per
268	    task
269	
270	-   **Formula:** Pass\@1 = (1/N) ∑ 1\[task\_i passes on ≥1 of k
271	    samples\]
272	
273	-   **Expected range:** 5--15% improvement signals real effect
274	
275	-   **If gate fails:** Stop and reassess before investing further GPU
276	    time
277	
278	5.2 Baselines
279	
280	  ------------------------------- ------------------------------------------ ---------------------------------
281	  **Condition**                   **Description**                            **Purpose**
282	  Vanilla model                   Qwen3.5-9B base (bfloat16, no adapter)     Lower bound
283	  RAG baseline                    Retrieved trajectory snippets in context   Context-space memory ceiling
284	  QLoRA (DeltaCoder warm-start)   Stage 1 adapter on same trajectory data    Weight-space upper bound
285	  Hypernetwork-generated          Stage 2 single forward pass                Production system quality check
286	  ------------------------------- ------------------------------------------ ---------------------------------
287	
288	5.3 Diagnostic Metrics (Non-Gating)
289	
290	-   **Adapter Frobenius norm \|\|**ΔW**\|\|\_F:** Must be meaningfully
291	    nonzero---confirms adapter modifies model behavior
292	
293	-   **Inter-adapter cosine diversity \> 0.1:** Confirms diversity, not
294	    mode collapse
295	
296	-   **Training loss convergence:** Should decrease without early plateau
297	
298	5.4 PBB-Inspired Procedural Encoding Test
299	
300	Beyond Pass\@1: generate an adapter from a trajectory containing a
301	specific algorithm, load it, and query with held-out inputs *not in the
302	trajectory and not in context*. If the adapter-loaded model executes the
303	procedure better than the base model on novel inputs, the adapter has
304	encoded procedural knowledge in weight space.
305	
306	6\. Inference Configuration
307	
308	6.1 Temperature Recalibration
309	
310	The current optimized temperature of 0.25 was derived on
311	Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct. DeltaCoder's model card documents 0.5 as the
312	hard minimum for multi-turn agentic use. After migration, re-run
313	scripts/optimization/run\_optimization.py (Bayesian optimization, 200+
314	trials) on the new stack.
315	
316	6.2 Sampling Defaults
317	
318	  -------------------- ----------------- ----------- ----------- --------------------------------------
319	  **Context**          **Temperature**   **Top-k**   **Top-p**   **Notes**
320	  Multi-turn agentic   0.6               20          0.95        DeltaCoder model card recommendation
321	  Deterministic/code   0.2--0.4          ---         ---         Lower for consistent output
322	  Exploration phases   0.7--0.8          30          0.95        Higher for diverse decomposition
323	  Repetition penalty   1.04              ---         ---         Bayesian-optimized on previous stack
324	  -------------------- ----------------- ----------- ----------- --------------------------------------
325	
326	6.3 Serving Architecture
327	
328	vLLM with S-LoRA unified paging enables serving with up to 8 concurrent
329	adapters. Single-GPU training coordinates via sleep/wake endpoints.
330	Multi-GPU uses pipeline parallelism (not tensor parallelism---vLLM issue
331	\#21471 documents LoRA corruption on PCIe without NVLink).
332

Now let me verify the complete pipeline by creating a concise summary with actual examples:

Based on my exploration of PR #19 in the rune project, here's a comprehensive report on the **data mining pipeline output format and schema**:

---

## Data Mining Pipeline Overview

PR #19 implements a complete training dataset pipeline for hypernetwork distillation with three stages:

1. **GitHub Mining** (`mine_github.py`) → Raw trajectories
2. **Normalization & Pair Extraction** (`d2l_data.py::normalize_mined_pairs`) → Training pairs
3. **Diff Compression** (`d2l_diff.py::compress_diff`) → Cleaned diffs
4. **Persistence** → JSONL files

---

## Output Format: Per-Step Training Pairs (JSONL)

**File Paths:**
- **Config**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/instructions/mining_repos.json`
- **Output location** (batch mode): `data/pairs/{repo_name}.jsonl` (one file per repo)
- **Data module**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py`
- **Mining module**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py`
- **Diff compression**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_diff.py`

### Record Schema (JSONL)

Each line in the JSONL output is a JSON object with:

```json
{
  "task_id": "pr_huggingface/transformers_15042",
  "activation_text": "## Task\nAdd support for flash attention...\n\n## Current Code\n--- src/attention.py ---\n-old impl\n+new impl\n\n## Review Feedback\nNeed error handling for edge cases",
  "teacher_text": "## Task\nAdd support for flash attention...\n\n## Current Code\n--- src/attention.py ---\n-old impl\n+new impl\n\n## Review Feedback\nNeed error handling for edge cases\n\n## Revision\n--- src/attention.py ---\n+fixed impl with error handling",
  "metadata": {
    "outcome": "merged",
    "step_index": 1,
    "language": "python",
    "source_task_id": "pr_huggingface/transformers_15042"
  }
}
```

### Field Descriptions

| Field | Type | Description |
|-------|------|-------------|
| `task_id` | string | Base PR identifier without step suffix (e.g., `pr_owner/repo_N`) |
| `activation_text` | string | **Student input** - task description + current code + review feedback (NO answer) |
| `teacher_text` | string | **Teacher output** - activation_text + revision/solution code |
| `metadata.outcome` | string | `"merged"` (success) or `"closed"` (failure) |
| `metadata.step_index` | int | Step number in the review cycle (0 = initial commit, 1+ = revisions) |
| `metadata.language` | string | Language tag from repos config (`"python"`, `"go"`, `"rust"`, etc.) |
| `metadata.source_task_id` | string | Original task_id (for split_by_task_id integrity) |

---

## Pipeline Stages

### Stage 1: Raw GitHub Mining → Trajectories

**Input**: PR metadata from GitHub API

**Output** (raw mined trajectory):
```json
{
  "task_id": "pr_owner/repo_42",
  "task_description": "PR title\n\nPR body text",
  "steps": [
    {"type": "commit", "description": "msg", "content": "--- file.py ---\n+code"},
    {"type": "review", "description": "Review", "content": "feedback text"},
    {"type": "commit", "description": "msg", "content": "--- file.py ---\n+revised"}
  ],
  "outcome": "merged"
}
```

**Key functions**:
- `mine_pr_diff_chains()` - extracts PR diffs + review comments
- `mine_issue_commit_chains()` - links issues to fixing commits
- `search_quality_prs()` - filters by min reviews/commits before mining

**Source**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py` (lines 120-234)

---

### Stage 2: Normalize to Training Pairs

**Function**: `normalize_mined_pairs(trajectory, compress=True, max_diff_lines=500, language=None)`

**Algorithm**: Groups steps into blocks, pairs each review-block with the following commit-block:

1. **Step 0 (Initial)**: Task + initial commit diff → training pair
2. **Step N**: Task + current code + review feedback + revision diff → training pair

**Example transformation**:

**Input trajectory**:
```
[commit(v1), review(feedback), commit(v2)]
```

**Output pairs** (2 records):
```
Pair 0: activation="Task\n## Implementation\n+v1"
        teacher="Task\n## Implementation\n+v1"

Pair 1: activation="Task\n## Current Code\n+v1\n## Review Feedback\nfeedback"
        teacher="Task\n## Current Code\n+v1\n## Review Feedback\nfeedback\n## Revision\n+v2"
```

**Source**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py` (lines 701-779)

---

### Stage 3: Diff Compression

**Function**: `compress_diff(content, max_lines=500)`

**Filters out**:
- Lockfiles: `package-lock.json`, `yarn.lock`, `poetry.lock`, `Cargo.lock`, etc.
- Generated code: `*_pb2.py`, `*.generated.ts`, etc.
- Binary/media: `.png`, `.jpg`, `.mp4`, `.woff`, `.pyc`, `.so`, etc.
- Build artifacts: `node_modules/`, `dist/`, `build/`, `.venv/`, `vendor/`, etc.

**Truncates** output to max_lines with marker: `"... (N lines truncated)"`

**Source**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_diff.py`

---

### Stage 4: Batch Mining & JSONL Output

**Script**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/scripts/mine_github.py`

**Usage**:
```bash
uv run python scripts/mine_github.py \
  --batch instructions/mining_repos.json \
  --output-dir data/pairs/ \
  --max-diff-lines 500
```

**Repos config** (`instructions/mining_repos.json`):
```json
{
  "defaults": {
    "max_prs": 50,
    "quality": true,
    "min_review_comments": 1,
    "min_commits": 2
  },
  "repos": [
    {"repo": "huggingface/transformers", "language": "python"},
    {"repo": "microsoft/TypeScript", "language": "typescript"},
    {"repo": "denoland/deno", "language": "rust"}
  ]
}
```

**Output**: One JSONL per repo
- `huggingface_transformers.jsonl` - ~100-200 pairs per 50 PRs
- `microsoft_TypeScript.jsonl`
- `denoland_deno.jsonl`

**Functions**:
- `_run_batch()` (lines 212-287) - orchestrates mining + pair extraction
- `_run_single()` (lines 138-209) - single repo mode

**Source**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/scripts/mine_github.py`

---

## Data Integrity Features

### Task ID Splitting (No Leakage)

All steps from the same PR use the **base task_id** without `_step_N` suffix:

```python
# All these pairs share task_id "pr_owner/repo_42":
pair_step0 = {"task_id": "pr_owner/repo_42", "metadata": {"step_index": 0}}
pair_step1 = {"task_id": "pr_owner/repo_42", "metadata": {"step_index": 1}}
```

The `split_by_task_id()` function keeps entire task families (all steps) in the same train/test split.

**Source**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py` (lines 629-655)

---

## Persistence Functions

### Save/Load JSONL

```python
from model_training.d2l_data import save_jsonl, load_jsonl

# Save
records = [...]  # list of pair dicts
save_jsonl(records, "data/pairs/repo_name.jsonl")

# Load
loaded = load_jsonl("data/pairs/repo_name.jsonl")
assert loaded == records  # round-trip guaranteed
```

## Summary

The **mining pipeline output is a JSONL file** where each line is a training pair with:
- **`activation_text`**: Prompt-only (no code answer) for student input
- **`teacher_text`**: Prompt + code answer for teacher training signal
- **`metadata`**: step_index, outcome, language, source_task_id for filtering/splitting

This format is directly consumable by training code via `load_jsonl()` and compatible with `split_by_task_id()` for train/test splitting with zero task-ID leakage across splits.

## Comprehensive Model Training Pipeline Analysis

### Overview
The `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training` directory contains a complete training infrastructure for LoRA fine-tuning and hypernetwork-based adapter generation. The pipeline supports three distinct training paths:

1. **QLoRA Training** (direct gradient-based fine-tuning)
2. **D2L Hypernetwork Training** (context distillation for rapid adapter generation)
3. **Adapter Merging** (TIES/DARE strategies for multi-adapter combination)

---

## 1. Python Source Files and Their Purposes

### Core Training Files

| File | Purpose | Key Classes/Functions |
|------|---------|----------------------|
| **d2l_train.py** | Main KL-divergence context distillation loop | `train_d2l_qwen3()`, `D2LTrainConfig`, `_training_step()`, `_compute_kl_ce_loss()` |
| **d2l_data.py** | Data pipeline for distillation | `format_for_distillation()`, `generate_needle_dataset()`, `save_jsonl()`, `load_jsonl()`, `split_by_task_id()` |
| **d2l_config.py** | Qwen3-Coder-Next architecture config | `get_d2l_qwen3_config()`, `build_qwen3_hypernet_config()` |
| **d2l_lora.py** | Functional LoRA injection via context manager | `apply_functional_lora()` (preserves autograd graph) |
| **d2l_probe.py** | Architecture probing & activation extraction | `probe_model()`, `extract_activations_with_model()`, `load_probe_cache()`, `save_probe_cache()` |
| **d2l_prep.py** | Data preparation (raw trajectories → JSONL) | `prepare_training_jsonl()` |
| **d2l_mining.py** | GitHub trajectory mining for training data | `mine_pr_diff_chains()`, `mine_issue_commit_chains()`, `search_quality_prs()` |
| **d2l_diff.py** | Diff compression (filters lockfiles, binaries) | `compress_diff()` |

| **peft_utils.py** | PEFT config builders | `build_qlora_config()`, `apply_lora_adapter()` |
| **config.py** | Training config validation (pure Python) | `get_training_config()`, `validate_config()` |

### Hypernetwork & Merging

| **merging.py** | Adapter combination strategies | `ties_merge()`, `dare_merge()`, `load_adapter_state_dict()` |

## 2. Training Pipeline Architecture

### High-Level Data Flow

```
Raw Trajectories (JSON)
    ↓
d2l_prep.py: prepare_training_jsonl()
    ↓
format_for_distillation() → JSONL with (activation_text, teacher_text, task_id)
    ↓
train_d2l_qwen3() loads JSONL
    ↓
For each record:
  1. Extract activations from activation_text via base model
  2. Hypernetwork (HyperLoRA) generates LoRA weights from activations
  3. Inject functional LoRA into base model
  4. Compute KL + CE loss between student & teacher on teacher_text
  5. Backward through hypernetwork parameters
    ↓
Trained Hypernetwork → Checkpoint
```

### Three Training Modes in `d2l_train.py`

1. **Dry-Run** (`--dry-run`): Validates tensor shapes with one forward pass, no optimizer step. Useful for CI validation.

2. **Smoke Test** (`--smoke-test`): Runs 5 training steps, verifies finite loss and decreasing trend. Fast validation that training loop works.

3. **Full Training** (default): Trains from JSONL dataset with:
   - Linear warmup + cosine annealing learning rate schedule
   - MLflow experiment tracking
   - Tiered checkpointing (lightweight every `checkpoint_every` steps, full every `full_checkpoint_every` steps)

---

## 3. Dataset Formats Supported

### D2L Training Data Format (JSONL)

Each record has:
```json
{
  "activation_text": "function definition + task description (NO answer tokens)",
  "teacher_text": "activation_text + correct answer",
  "task_id": "unique identifier for train/test splitting"
}
```

### Expected Record Counts

- **Needle Dataset** (synthetic, CI-only): 12 templates + 12 parameter sets = deterministic records
- **Real Trajectories**: From `format_for_distillation()` which requires:
  - `task_id`, `task_description`, `outcome` == "success"
  - `steps[]` with at least one step where `tests_passed == True`

### Data Validation

`split_by_task_id()` ensures zero task_id leakage between train/test splits (critical for distillation fidelity).

---

## 4. Training Configuration Options

### D2LTrainConfig (Pydantic Model in d2l_train.py)

| Parameter | Default | Range/Type | Purpose |
|-----------|---------|-----------|---------|
| `base_model_name` | `"Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next"` | str | HuggingFace model ID |
| `sakana_checkpoint_path` | required | str | Path to pretrained hypernetwork checkpoint |
| `num_steps` | 100 | int > 0 | Total training steps |
| `lr` | 2e-4 | float > 0 | AdamW learning rate |
| `alpha` | 0.5 | [0.0, 1.0] | KL vs CE blending (1.0=pure KL) |
| `temperature` | 2.0 | float > 0 | Softmax temperature for KL |
| `checkpoint_every` | 100 | int | Lightweight checkpoint interval |
| `full_checkpoint_every` | 500 | int | Full checkpoint (with optimizer) interval |
| `checkpoint_dir` | `"./checkpoints"` | str | Output directory |
| `experiment_name` | `"d2l-qwen3"` | str | MLflow experiment name |
| `grad_clip` | 1.0 | float | Gradient clipping max norm |
| `warmup_steps` | 10 | int | Linear warmup duration |
| `lora_r` | 8 | int | LoRA adapter rank |
| `max_length` | 512 | int | Tokenizer max sequence length |
| `dry_run` | False | bool | Validate shapes only |
| `smoke_test` | False | bool | Run 5 steps + verify loss trend |
| `dataset_path` | None | str | Path to training JSONL |

### QLoRA Config (config.py)

| Parameter | Default | Range | Purpose |
|-----------|---------|-------|---------|
| `task_type` | required | str | Task category |
| `rank` | 64 | (0, 256] | LoRA rank |
| `alpha` | 2*rank | N/A | Computed as 2×rank |
| `epochs` | 3 | (0, 100] | Training epochs |
| `learning_rate` | 2e-4 | (0, 1) | Optimizer LR |
| `warmup_ratio` | 0.03 | N/A | Fixed ratio |
| `target_modules` | ["q_proj", "v_proj"] | N/A | LoRA target layers |
| `dropout` | 0.1 | N/A | LoRA dropout |
| `per_device_train_batch_size` | 1 | N/A | Batch size (QLoRA) |
| `gradient_accumulation_steps` | 4 | N/A | Accumulation steps |

### HypernetConfig (for Qwen3-Coder-Next)

Built by `build_qwen3_hypernet_config()`:
- **layer_indices**: [3, 7, 11, 15, 19, 23, 27, 31, 35, 39, 43, 47] (12 full-attention layers only)
- **latent_size**: 512
- **target_modules**: ["q_proj", "v_proj"] (GQA key-value projections)
- **feature_sizes**: Per-module in/out dimensions (from probe cache or fallback to hidden_size=2048)
- **aggregator_config**: Perceiver configuration from Sakana checkpoint

## 5. Training Scripts and Entry Points

### Primary Entry Points

**D2L Training** (main hypernetwork training):
```bash
uv run python -m model_training.d2l_train \
  --sakana-checkpoint-path <path> \
  --num-steps 1000 \
  --lr 2e-4 \
  --dataset path/to/train.jsonl
```

**Data Preparation**:
```bash
uv run python -m model_training.d2l_prep \
  traj1.json traj2.json -o train.jsonl
```

**QLoRA Training** (via trainer.py):
```python
from model_training.trainer import train_qlora
adapter_path = train_qlora(
  session_id="sess-123",
  adapter_id="adapter-001",
  output_dir="./adapters",
  rank=64,
  epochs=3
)
```

### E2E Smoke Test

**File**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/scripts/e2e_training_smoke.py`

Three-section integration test:
1. **Section 1**: LoRA fine-tuning via functional injection - trains model to memorize specific completion
2. **Section 2**: Validates shift-aware loss computation (`_compute_kl_ce_loss` with causal LM offset)
3. **Section 3**: TIES merge of two adapters - verifies both behaviors retained after merging

Uses HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-135M (~270MB, auto-downloaded).

### Key Test Patterns

1. **GPU-Optional Tests**: `@requires_torch` decorator skips tests if torch unavailable
2. **Mocking**: GitHub API, safetensors library mocked for CPU-only CI
3. **Shape Validation**: Extensive checks that tensor dimensions flow correctly through hypernetwork
4. **Dtype Preservation**: TIES/DARE merges preserve bfloat16, float16 through computation
5. **Determinism**: DARE merge seeding for reproducibility
6. **Data Leakage**: Task-ID level splitting verified (no overlap between train/test)

## 7. Capabilities Implemented vs Missing

### ✅ Fully Implemented

**Training Infrastructure**:
- [x] D2L KL-divergence distillation with blended CE loss
- [x] Functional LoRA injection (preserves autograd to hypernetwork)
- [x] Linear warmup + cosine annealing LR schedule
- [x] Gradient clipping and MLflow tracking
- [x] Tiered checkpointing (lightweight + full)
- [x] Smoke test & dry-run validation modes
- [x] Probe-based feature dimension caching

**Data Pipeline**:
- [x] Trajectory mining from GitHub (PR chains, issue chains)
- [x] Distillation record generation (activation/teacher split)
- [x] Needle dataset generation (synthetic, deterministic)
- [x] JSONL I/O with task-ID-level train/test splitting
- [x] Diff compression (lockfile/binary filtering)

**Adapter Management**:
- [x] QLoRA fine-tuning with NF4 quantization
- [x] TIES-Merging (trim-elect-sign-disjoint)
- [x] DARE-Merging (drop-and-rescale)
- [x] Hypernetwork perceiver (DocToLoraHypernetwork)
- [x] Sakana integration with weight transfer
- [x] Safetensors adapter persistence

**Architecture Probing**:
- [x] Dynamic layer discovery (full vs linear attention)
- [x] Per-projection dimension caching
- [x] Cache invalidation & warnings

### ⚠️ Partially Implemented / Known Gaps

**QLoRA Path**:
- [ ] Batch dataset loading (current: single-trajectory-per-call)
- [ ] Distributed training (single-GPU only)
- [ ] Model-parallel inference

**Probe System**:
- [ ] Probe cache currently requires manual probe_model() + save_probe_cache() calls
- [ ] No automatic cache invalidation on model weight changes
- [ ] Placeholder dimensions used in smoke tests (warnings logged)

**Training Observability**:
- [ ] Limited loss decomposition (only KL vs CE, no per-layer analysis)
- [ ] No gradient statistics logging
- [ ] No validation set loss (training-only)

### ❌ Not Implemented / Out of Scope

- Distributed/multi-GPU training
- Adapter quantization post-training
- Dynamic batch sizing based on token budget
- Fine-grained task-level loss weighting
- Knowledge distillation with auxiliary heads
- Streaming dataset loading from S3/HDFS

## 8. GPU Import Pattern (INFRA-05)

All heavy imports (torch, transformers, peft, safetensors) are deferred to function bodies:

```python
# Module level: SAFE to import in CI
from model_training.config import get_training_config

# Function body: GPU deps deferred
def train_qlora(...):
    import torch  # ← only imports when function called
    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM
    ...
```

This allows CPU-only test environments to import the entire package without requiring GPU libraries.

## Summary

The model-training library provides a **complete, modular training infrastructure** for fine-tuning language models via:

1. **QLoRA path**: Traditional gradient-based fine-tuning with quantization
2. **Hypernetwork path**: Fast (<1s) adapter generation via context distillation
3. **Merging path**: Combination strategies (TIES/DARE) for multi-adapter scenarios

All components follow the **INFRA-05 GPU import deferral pattern**, enabling CPU-only CI environments while supporting GPU training. The test suite (150+ tests) covers data pipelines, loss computation, checkpointing, and adapter merging with extensive dtype and shape validation.

1	"""QLoRA training orchestrator.
2	
3	All GPU-dependent imports (datasets, transformers, trl, torch) are deferred
4	inside function bodies to ensure CPU-only importability (INFRA-05).
5	
6	Module-level imports: stdlib only.
7	"""
8	
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import hashlib
12	import os
13	from datetime import datetime, timezone
14	from pathlib import Path
15	
16	
17	def train_qlora(
18	    session_id: str,
19	    adapter_id: str,
20	    output_dir: str,
21	    *,
22	    base_model_id: str | None = None,
23	    task_type: str = "code-gen",
24	    rank: int = 64,
25	    alpha: int = 128,
26	    epochs: int = 3,
27	    learning_rate: float = 2e-4,
28	) -> str:
29	    """Train a QLoRA adapter from a recorded coding trajectory.
30	
31	    Orchestrates the full training pipeline: load trajectory, format as SFT
32	    messages, build dataset, load model with NF4 quantization, train with SFT,
33	    and save the adapter to output_dir.
34	
35	    All GPU imports are deferred to this function body; the module is safe to
36	    import in CPU-only environments.
37	
38	    Args:
39	        session_id: Trajectory session ID to train from.
40	        adapter_id: Unique identifier for the resulting adapter.
41	        output_dir: Directory to save the trained adapter weights.
42	        base_model_id: HuggingFace model ID. Defaults to RUNE_BASE_MODEL env var
43	            or "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct".
44	        task_type: Task category (e.g. 'code-gen', 'bug-fix').
45	        rank: LoRA rank.
46	        alpha: LoRA alpha scaling factor.
47	        epochs: Number of training epochs.
48	        learning_rate: Optimizer learning rate.
49	
50	    Returns:
51	        output_dir path where the adapter was saved.
52	
53	    Raises:
54	        FileNotFoundError: If the trajectory file does not exist.
55	        ValueError: If the trajectory is not successful or has no SFT messages.
56	    """
57	    # Deferred GPU imports — all in function body for CPU-only importability
58	    import torch
59	    from datasets import Dataset
60	    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer, BitsAndBytesConfig
61	    from trl import SFTConfig, SFTTrainer
62	
63	    from model_training.peft_utils import build_qlora_config
64	    from model_training.trajectory import format_for_sft, load_trajectory
65	
66	    # Resolve model ID — read env var inside function body for monkeypatch testability
67	    model_id = base_model_id or os.environ.get(
68	        "RUNE_BASE_MODEL", "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct"
69	    )
70	
71	    # Load and format trajectory
72	    trajectory = load_trajectory(session_id)  # raises FileNotFoundError if missing
73	    messages = format_for_sft(trajectory)
74	    if not messages:
75	        raise ValueError(
76	            f"Trajectory {session_id} is not successful or has no SFT messages"
77	        )
78	
79	    # Build dataset
80	    dataset = Dataset.from_list([{"messages": messages}])
81	
82	    # NF4 quantization config (bfloat16 compute dtype prevents silent NaN loss)
83	    bnb_config = BitsAndBytesConfig(
84	        load_in_4bit=True,
85	        bnb_4bit_quant_type="nf4",
86	        bnb_4bit_compute_dtype=torch.bfloat16,
87	        bnb_4bit_use_double_quant=True,
88	    )
89	
90	    # Load model and tokenizer
91	    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
92	        model_id,
93	        quantization_config=bnb_config,
94	        device_map="auto",
95	    )
96	    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_id)
97	
98	    # Build LoRA config (bfloat16 is set in BitsAndBytesConfig, not LoraConfig)
99	    lora_config = build_qlora_config(
100	        rank=rank,
101	        alpha=alpha,
102	        target_modules=["q_proj", "v_proj"],
103	        dropout=0.1,
104	    )
105	
106	    # Training arguments
107	    training_args = SFTConfig(
108	        output_dir=output_dir,
109	        num_train_epochs=epochs,
110	        learning_rate=learning_rate,
111	        warmup_ratio=0.03,
112	        lr_scheduler_type="cosine",
113	        bf16=True,
114	        per_device_train_batch_size=1,
115	        gradient_accumulation_steps=1,
116	        save_strategy="no",
117	        logging_steps=1,
118	        report_to="none",
119	        eval_strategy="no",
120	    )
121	
122	    # Create and run trainer
123	    trainer = SFTTrainer(
124	        model=model,
125	        args=training_args,
126	        train_dataset=dataset,
127	        peft_config=lora_config,
128	        processing_class=tokenizer,
129	    )
130	    trainer.train()
131	
132	    # Save adapter weights only (PEFT-aware: safetensors + adapter_config.json)
133	    trainer.save_model(output_dir)
134	
135	    return output_dir
136	
137	
138	def train_and_register(
139	    session_id: str,
140	    adapter_id: str,
141	    *,
142	    base_model_id: str | None = None,
143	    task_type: str = "code-gen",
144	    rank: int = 64,
145	    alpha: int = 128,
146	    epochs: int = 3,
147	    learning_rate: float = 2e-4,
148	    database_url: str | None = None,
149	) -> str:
150	    """Train a QLoRA adapter and register it in the AdapterRegistry.
151	
152	    Combines train_qlora() with AdapterRegistry.store() to produce a fully
153	    registered adapter ready for vLLM serving.
154	
155	    Args:
156	        session_id: Trajectory session ID to train from.
157	        adapter_id: Unique identifier for the resulting adapter.
158	        base_model_id: HuggingFace model ID. Defaults to RUNE_BASE_MODEL env var.
159	        task_type: Task category (e.g. 'code-gen', 'bug-fix').
160	        rank: LoRA rank.
161	        alpha: LoRA alpha scaling factor.
162	        epochs: Number of training epochs.
163	        learning_rate: Optimizer learning rate.
164	        database_url: SQLAlchemy database URL. Defaults to RUNE_DATABASE_URL env var
165	            or "sqlite:///{home}/.rune/rune.db".
166	
167	    Returns:
168	        adapter_id of the registered adapter.
169	
170	    Raises:
171	        FileNotFoundError: If the trajectory file does not exist.
172	        ValueError: If the trajectory is not successful or has no SFT messages.
173	    """
174	    from adapter_registry.models import AdapterRecord
175	    from adapter_registry.registry import AdapterRegistry
176	    from sqlalchemy import create_engine
177	
178	    # Resolve adapter output dir — read env var inside function body
179	    adapter_base = os.environ.get("RUNE_ADAPTER_DIR")
180	    if adapter_base:
181	        adapter_dir = Path(adapter_base) / adapter_id
182	    else:
183	        adapter_dir = Path.home() / ".rune" / "adapters" / adapter_id
184	    adapter_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
185	
186	    # Resolve model ID inside function body for testability
187	    model_id = base_model_id or os.environ.get(
188	        "RUNE_BASE_MODEL", "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct"
189	    )
190	
191	    output_dir = str(adapter_dir)
192	    train_qlora(
193	        session_id=session_id,
194	        adapter_id=adapter_id,
195	        output_dir=output_dir,
196	        base_model_id=model_id,
197	        task_type=task_type,
198	        rank=rank,
199	        alpha=alpha,
200	        epochs=epochs,
201	        learning_rate=learning_rate,
202	    )
203	
204	    # Compute file hash and size from the saved safetensors file
205	    adapter_file = adapter_dir / "adapter_model.safetensors"
206	    file_hash = hashlib.sha256(adapter_file.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
207	    file_size_bytes = adapter_file.stat().st_size
208	
209	    # Build the adapter record
210	    record = AdapterRecord(
211	        id=adapter_id,
212	        version=1,
213	        task_type=task_type,
214	        base_model_id=model_id,
215	        rank=rank,
216	        created_at=datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc).isoformat(),
217	        file_path=output_dir,
218	        file_hash=file_hash,
219	        file_size_bytes=file_size_bytes,
220	        source="qlora",
221	        session_id=session_id,
222	    )
223	
224	    # Resolve DB URL inside function body for testability
225	    db_url = database_url or os.environ.get(
226	        "RUNE_DATABASE_URL",
227	        f"sqlite:///{Path.home() / '.rune' / 'rune.db'}",
228	    )
229	    engine = create_engine(db_url)
230	    registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
231	    registry.store(record)
232	
233	    return adapter_id
234

1	"""KL-divergence context distillation training loop for Qwen3-Coder-Next.
2	
3	Assembles all Phase 25-28 components (config, data pipeline, activation
4	extraction, weight transfer, functional LoRA injection) into a complete
5	distillation training script.
6	
7	Three execution modes:
8	- dry-run: loads real base model + hypernet, validates tensor shapes, exits
9	- smoke-test: 5 training steps, verifies finite loss and decreasing trend
10	- full: trains from JSONL dataset with tiered checkpointing and MLflow tracking
11	
12	All heavy GPU imports (torch, transformers, peft) are deferred to function
13	bodies per INFRA-05 project convention.
14	
15	Usage:
16	    uv run python -m model_training.d2l_train --dry-run
17	    uv run python -m model_training.d2l_train --smoke-test
18	    uv run python -m model_training.d2l_train --dataset path/to/train.jsonl
19	"""
20	
21	from __future__ import annotations
22	
23	import logging
24	import os
25	from pathlib import Path
26	from typing import Any
27	
28	from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, field_validator
29	
30	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
31	
32	__all__ = ["train_d2l_qwen3", "D2LTrainConfig"]
33	
34	
35	def _require_probe_cache(model_name: str) -> None:
36	    """Raise RuntimeError if probe cache is absent for *model_name*.
37	
38	    Called before build_qwen3_hypernet_config so training never proceeds with
39	    placeholder feature_sizes that produce incorrect LoRA dimensions.
40	
41	    Defers the import of load_probe_cache so GPU-free test environments are
42	    not forced to import the full probe module at module load time.
43	
44	    Args:
45	        model_name: Canonical model identifier for cache lookup and error message.
46	
47	    Raises:
48	        RuntimeError: If no probe cache is found for model_name.
49	    """
50	    from model_training.d2l_probe import load_probe_cache  # noqa: PLC0415
51	
52	    if load_probe_cache(model_name) is not None:
53	        return
54	    msg = (
55	        f"Probe cache not found for '{model_name}' — "
56	        "run probe_model() and save_probe_cache() before training. "
57	        "Training with placeholder feature_sizes produces incorrect LoRA "
58	        "dimensions."
59	    )
60	    raise RuntimeError(msg)
61	
62	
63	class D2LTrainConfig(BaseModel):
64	    """Pydantic model for D2L training hyperparameters.
65	
66	    Enables validation, JSON serialization (for checkpoint storage), and
67	    `.model_dump()` for MLflow experiment logging.
68	
69	    Attributes:
70	        base_model_name: HuggingFace model name for the student/teacher base.
71	        sakana_checkpoint_path: Path to the Sakana hypernet checkpoint.
72	        num_steps: Total training steps.
73	        lr: Learning rate for AdamW optimizer.
74	        alpha: Blending weight for KL vs CE loss (1.0 = pure KL, 0.0 = pure CE).
75	        temperature: Softmax temperature for KL divergence computation.
76	        checkpoint_every: Steps between lightweight checkpoint saves.
77	        full_checkpoint_every: Steps between full checkpoint saves (incl. optimizer).
78	        checkpoint_dir: Directory for checkpoint output.
79	        experiment_name: MLflow experiment name.
80	        dry_run: If True, validate tensor shapes then exit.

1	"""Config helpers for Qwen3-Coder-Next hypernetwork training.
2	
3	Requires transformers>=5.0 for Qwen3NextConfig (hybrid linear/full attention
4	architecture). transformers 5.3.0 is installed in this project.
5	
6	All heavy imports (transformers, ctx_to_lora, peft) are deferred to function
7	bodies per project convention (INFRA-05) to avoid GPU imports at module level.
8	"""
9	
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	import logging
13	from typing import Any
14	
15	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
16	
17	__all__ = ["get_d2l_qwen3_config", "build_qwen3_hypernet_config"]
18	
19	
20	def get_d2l_qwen3_config() -> dict[str, Any]:
21	    """Return Qwen3-Coder-Next architecture dimensions without loading model weights.
22	
23	    Uses Qwen3NextConfig defaults which exactly match Qwen3-Coder-Next specs:
24	    - hidden_size: 2048
25	    - num_hidden_layers: 48 (12 full_attention + 36 linear_attention)
26	    - num_attention_heads: 16 (Q heads), num_key_value_heads: 2 (GQA KV)
27	    - head_dim: 256
28	    - full_attention layer indices: [3, 7, 11, 15, 19, 23, 27, 31, 35, 39, 43, 47]
29	    - vocab_size: 151936
30	    - model_type: "qwen3_next"
31	
32	    Returns:
33	        Dict with keys: hidden_size, num_hidden_layers, num_attention_heads,
34	        num_key_value_heads, head_dim, attention_layer_indices, vocab_size,
35	        model_type.
36	    """
37	    from transformers import Qwen3NextConfig  # noqa: PLC0415
38	
39	    cfg = Qwen3NextConfig()
40	    layer_types: list[str] = cfg.layer_types or []
41	    attention_layer_indices = [
42	        i for i, t in enumerate(layer_types) if t == "full_attention"
43	    ]
44	    return {
45	        "hidden_size": cfg.hidden_size,
46	        "num_hidden_layers": cfg.num_hidden_layers,
47	        "num_attention_heads": cfg.num_attention_heads,
48	        "num_key_value_heads": cfg.num_key_value_heads,
49	        "head_dim": cfg.head_dim,
50	        "attention_layer_indices": attention_layer_indices,
51	        "vocab_size": cfg.vocab_size,
52	        "model_type": cfg.model_type,
53	    }
54	
55	
56	def build_qwen3_hypernet_config(
57	    lora_r: int = 8,
58	    target_modules: list[str] | None = None,
59	    aggregator_config: Any = None,
60	) -> Any:
61	    """Construct HypernetConfig targeting Qwen3-Coder-Next attention layers.
62	
63	    Discovers full_attention layer indices dynamically from Qwen3NextConfig.layer_types.
64	    Result has exactly 12 layer indices matching the Qwen3-Coder-Next architecture.
65	
66	    Phase 26 probe cache integration: if a probe cache exists for
67	    QWEN3_NEXT_CANONICAL_NAME, uses real per-projection in/out dimensions for
68	    feature_sizes. Falls back to hidden_size placeholder when no cache is found
69	    (e.g., in CI where the model has not been probed).
70	
71	    Args:
72	        lora_r: LoRA rank for the adapter. Defaults to 8.
73	        target_modules: LoRA target module names. Defaults to ["q_proj", "v_proj"].
74	        aggregator_config: Perceiver aggregator config from a Sakana checkpoint.
75	            If None (default / Phase 25 CI), HypernetConfig is built with
76	            aggregator_config=None as placeholder. Phase 29 populates this via
77	            get_aggregator_config() with a loaded model.
78	
79	    Returns:
80	        HypernetConfig with layer_indices set to the 12 full_attention indices
81	        and base_hidden_size=2048.
82	    """
83	    from ctx_to_lora.modeling.hypernet import HypernetConfig  # noqa: PLC0415
84	    from peft import LoraConfig  # noqa: PLC0415
85	    from transformers import Qwen3NextConfig  # noqa: PLC0415
86	
87	    if target_modules is None:
88	        target_modules = ["q_proj", "v_proj"]
89	
90	    cfg = Qwen3NextConfig()
91	    layer_types: list[str] = cfg.layer_types or []
92	    layer_indices = [
93	        i for i, t in enumerate(layer_types) if t == "full_attention"
94	    ]  # Always [3, 7, 11, 15, 19, 23, 27, 31, 35, 39, 43, 47]
95	
96	    lora_config = LoraConfig(
97	        r=lora_r,
98	        lora_alpha=lora_r * 2,
99	        target_modules=target_modules,
100	        lora_dropout=0.0,
101	        bias="none",
102	        task_type="CAUSAL_LM",
103	    )

106	        QWEN3_NEXT_CANONICAL_NAME,

111	    if cache is not None:
112	        in_sizes = {mod: cache["feature_sizes"][mod]["in"] for mod in target_modules}
113	        out_sizes = {mod: cache["feature_sizes"][mod]["out"] for mod in target_modules}
114	        feature_sizes: tuple[dict[str, int], dict[str, int]] = (in_sizes, out_sizes)
115	        logger.info("Using probe cache feature_sizes for %s", QWEN3_NEXT_CANONICAL_NAME)
116	    else:
117	        hidden: int = cfg.hidden_size or 2048
118	        _placeholder: dict[str, int] = dict.fromkeys(target_modules, hidden)
119	        feature_sizes = (_placeholder, dict.fromkeys(target_modules, hidden))
120	        logger.warning(
121	            "No probe cache for '%s' — using hidden_size=%d as placeholder. "
122	            "Run probe_model() and save_probe_cache() to set real dimensions.",
123	            QWEN3_NEXT_CANONICAL_NAME,
124	            cfg.hidden_size,
125	        )
126	
127	    return HypernetConfig(
128	        latent_size=512,

## Fine-Tuning Readiness Assessment

### Data Pipeline: READY
The mining pipeline (PR #19) outputs JSONL with `activation_text`/`teacher_text`/`task_id` fields, which is exactly what `d2l_train.py` consumes via `load_jsonl()`. The `split_by_task_id()` function prevents data leakage. Diff compression filters noise. This path is end-to-end connected.

### Hypernetwork (Stage 2) Training: MOSTLY READY
- KL+CE blended loss with configurable alpha ✅
- Sakana aggregator weight transfer ✅  
- Functional LoRA injection preserving autograd ✅
- Probe-based feature dimension discovery ✅
- MLflow tracking + tiered checkpointing ✅
- Dry-run and smoke-test validation modes ✅

### Evaluation: READY
Benchmark framework in `scripts/eval/` supports HumanEval+, MBPP+, BigCodeBench with pass@1 comparison between base and LoRA conditions. Kill-switch gate can be measured.

### TIES/DARE Merging: READY

---

## Gaps Requiring Attention

### 1. No DeltaCoder Warm-Start (Critical - Strategy Section 2.1)

`trainer.py:91` loads a fresh base model via `AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained()`. The strategy requires loading `danielcherubini/Qwen3.5-DeltaCoder-9B` via `PeftModel.from_pretrained()` and continuing training on those existing LoRA weights. This is the entire premise of Stage 1 — skipping initial convergence by starting from DeltaCoder's pretrained adapter.

### 2. Target Modules Too Narrow (Critical - Strategy Section 2.3)

Both `trainer.py:102` and `d2l_config.py:88` default to `["q_proj", "v_proj"]` only. The strategy specifies **12 module types** across GDN, attention, and MLP:
- GDN: `in_proj_qkv`, `in_proj_z`, `in_proj_b`, `in_proj_a`, `out_proj`
- Attention: `q_proj`, `k_proj`, `v_proj`, `o_proj`
- MLP: `gate_proj`, `up_proj`, `down_proj`

With only q/v projections, the adapter has far less capacity than the strategy calls for.

### 3. No Assistant-Token-Only Loss Masking (Strategy Section 2.2)

The strategy requires: *"Mask system/user turns; compute loss only on model outputs."* Grep for any `ignore_index` or label masking returned zero results. The SFTTrainer in `trainer.py` doesn't configure this, and the D2L loop in `d2l_train.py` doesn't appear to mask non-assistant tokens either.

### 4. QLoRA Hyperparameter Mismatches (Strategy Section 2.2)

| Parameter | Strategy | Code (`trainer.py`) |
|-----------|----------|---------------------|
| Base model | Qwen3.5-9B | `Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct` |
| Gradient accum | 16-32 | 1 |
| LR schedule | Constant + warmup | Cosine |
| Attention impl | `eager` (explicit) | Not set (defaults to flash) |
| LoRA rank | 64 (DeltaCoder) | 64 ✅ |

### 5. Model Architecture Drift

The D2L config (`d2l_config.py`) targets **Qwen3-Coder-Next** (48 layers, hybrid linear/full attention), while the strategy document targets **Qwen3.5-9B** (32 layers). The QLoRA trainer still defaults to the old **Qwen2.5-Coder-7B**. These need to be aligned to a single target model.

---

## Recommendation

The **Stage 2 (hypernetwork) path is close to launch-ready** — the training loop, data pipeline, and evaluation framework are connected.

The **Stage 1 (QLoRA) path has 3 critical gaps** before it can produce the adapter corpus that Stage 2 needs:
1. Add DeltaCoder warm-start (load existing adapter, set `requires_grad=True`, continue training)
2. Expand target modules to the full 12-module GDN list
3. Add assistant-token-only loss masking

Plus the hyperparameter alignment (gradient accumulation, LR schedule, attention impl, model ID).

Would you like me to start addressing these gaps?

## Summary: trl SFTTrainer Loss Masking Analysis

Based on my thorough exploration of your rune codebase and trl documentation, here's what I found:

### **TRL Version Constraint**
Your project specifies **trl >= 0.16.0** in `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/pyproject.toml` (line 47, under gpu optional dependencies). This is also reflected in `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/pyproject.toml` (line 21).

### **Current Implementation**
Your QLoRA trainer in `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/trainer.py` currently:
- Uses `SFTConfig` and `SFTTrainer` from trl
- Constructs messages via `format_for_sft()` which produces a 3-message list: `[{"role": "system", ...}, {"role": "user", ...}, {"role": "assistant", ...}]`
- Passes the dataset to SFTTrainer with `processing_class=tokenizer` (line 128)
- **Does NOT currently use any loss masking parameters** — it relies on trl's defaults

### **Native Loss Masking Approaches Available in trl >= 0.16.0**

trl provides **two native approaches** for assistant-token-only loss masking:

#### **1. `assistant_only_loss=True` (Recommended for Conversational Data)**
```python
from trl import SFTConfig, SFTTrainer

training_args = SFTConfig(
    output_dir=output_dir,
    # ... other args ...
    assistant_only_loss=True,  # Add this line
)

trainer = SFTTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=dataset,
    peft_config=lora_config,
    processing_class=tokenizer,
)
```

**How it works:**
- Computes loss **only on assistant message tokens**, ignoring system and user messages
- Automatically identifies assistant tokens using chat templates with special generation markers
- Requires your model's tokenizer to have a compatible `chat_template`

**Your status:** The Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct model (your default in trainer.py line 68) has built-in chat template support, so this should work out-of-the-box.

#### **2. `completion_only_loss=True` (For Prompt-Completion Format)**
```python
training_args = SFTConfig(
    completion_only_loss=True,  # (This is actually the DEFAULT)
)
```

**How it works:**
- Designed for prompt→completion pairs where you only want loss on the completion
- This is already the **default behavior** in SFTTrainer
- You can explicitly set `completion_only_loss=False` to train on full sequences

**Your status:** Since you're using conversational messages (not prompt-completion pairs), `assistant_only_loss=True` is more semantically appropriate.

#### **3. DataCollatorForLanguageModeling (Lower-Level Control)**
If you need more granular control, you can use a custom data collator:

```python
from trl.trainer.sft_trainer import DataCollatorForLanguageModeling

collator = DataCollatorForLanguageModeling(
    pad_token_id=tokenizer.pad_token_id,
    max_length=2048,
    completion_only_loss=True,
    padding_free=False,
)

trainer = SFTTrainer(
    # ... 
    data_collator=collator,
)
```

### **Existing Usage in Your Codebase**
No existing usage of loss masking parameters found:
- No `DataCollatorForCompletionOnlyLM` usage
- No `completion_only`, `response_template`, or `assistant_only_loss` parameters currently configured
- No custom loss masking implementation

### **Recommendation for Your QLoRA Pipeline**

To mask loss to assistant tokens only, modify `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/trainer.py` line 107-120:

```python
training_args = SFTConfig(
    output_dir=output_dir,
    num_train_epochs=epochs,
    learning_rate=learning_rate,
    warmup_ratio=0.03,
    lr_scheduler_type="cosine",
    bf16=True,
    per_device_train_batch_size=1,
    gradient_accumulation_steps=1,
    save_strategy="no",
    logging_steps=1,
    report_to="none",
    eval_strategy="no",
    assistant_only_loss=True,  # ← Add this line
)
```

This approach:
- ✅ Works natively with trl >= 0.16.0
- ✅ Automatically uses your model's chat template to identify assistant tokens
- ✅ Prevents gradient flow through system/user tokens
- ✅ Aligns with your current message format (role-based conversations)
```

## Summary

I've completed a thorough exploration of the rune project's QLoRA training pipeline and identified exactly what code changes are needed to integrate DeltaCoder warm-start. Here's what I found:

### Current Architecture

The project has a clean, modular training pipeline:
- **`trainer.py`**: Orchestrates QLoRA training (loads model, builds fresh LoRA config, trains via SFTTrainer)
- **`peft_utils.py`**: PEFT utilities (builds LoRA configs, applies adapters)
- **`training-svc`**: FastAPI service that dispatches training jobs
- **Test coverage**: Uses CPU-safe mocking patterns for GPU libraries

### Required Changes (6 modifications)

**1. New function in `peft_utils.py`** — `load_peft_adapter()`
   - Load pre-trained PEFT adapters via `PeftModel.from_pretrained()`
   - Unfreeze adapter parameters with `requires_grad=True`
   - Mirror the existing pattern: deferred imports, docstring style

**2. Extend `train_qlora()` signature in `trainer.py`**
   - Add optional `warm_start_adapter_id` parameter (keyword-only)
   - Backward compatible: defaults to `None`

**3. Add conditional logic in `train_qlora()`**
   - If `warm_start_adapter_id` provided: load pre-trained adapter, skip fresh LoRA config
   - Otherwise: existing behavior (fresh config)

**4. Update SFTTrainer call in `train_qlora()`**
   - Only pass `peft_config` if not warm-starting
   - Use kwargs dict to handle conditional parameter

**5. Update `train_and_register()` to pass through `warm_start_adapter_id`**
   - Add parameter to signature
   - Pass to `train_qlora()`

**6. Update training service API schema and handler**
   - Add optional `warm_start_adapter_id` field to `LoraTrainingRequest`
   - Update `_run_training_job()` and endpoint to pass parameter through

### Key Technical Insights

- **DeltaCoder's target modules** are already configured in its `adapter_config.json`, so no custom `target_modules` needed
- **PEFT's behavior**: loading a pre-trained adapter creates a `PeftModel` that SFTTrainer recognizes—no `peft_config` parameter needed
- **Parameter unfreezing**: The comment in `sakana_d2l.py` shows rune already uses `requires_grad=True/False` patterns elsewhere
- **CPU importability**: All changes follow the existing pattern of deferred GPU imports inside function bodies

## Summary

I've completed a comprehensive exploration of the model training probe system in the Rune codebase. Here are my key findings:

### **What the Probe System Does (Dynamic Discovery)**

The `d2l_probe.py` module contains a **model-agnostic discovery system** that can probe any transformer model:

1. **`probe_model(model)`** - Discovers all attention layers (those with q_proj/k_proj/v_proj/o_proj children) via `named_modules()` and captures their weight dimensions
2. **`save_probe_cache(model_name, probe_result)`** - Persists results to JSON at `~/.cache/rune/probes/`
3. **`load_probe_cache(model_name)`** - Retrieves cached probe data for a model
4. **`extract_activations_with_model()`** - Uses cached layer indices to extract per-layer activations

### **What's Currently Hardcoded (Qwen3-Coder-Next Only)**

**In `d2l_config.py`:**
- Uses `Qwen3NextConfig()` directly to get architecture (hybrid 48-layer model with 12 full-attention layers at indices [3, 7, 11, 15, 19, 23, 27, 31, 35, 39, 43, 47])
- `hidden_size=2048`, `vocab_size=151936`, `num_attention_heads=16`, `num_key_value_heads=2`
- Layer discovery uses `Qwen3NextConfig.layer_types` to find `"full_attention"` indices
- `build_qwen3_hypernet_config()` is hardcoded to this specific model

**In `d2l_train.py`:**
- Default base_model: `"Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next"`
- `_require_probe_cache(QWEN3_NEXT_CANONICAL_NAME)` - guards all training with a hardcoded string

**In tests:**
- Expect exactly 12 attention indices and hidden_size=2048

### **Key Insight: Mismatch Between Capability and Implementation**

The probe system **CAN dynamically discover everything about any model**, but:
- Only Qwen3-Coder-Next has a probe cache
- Config building is hardcoded to use `Qwen3NextConfig`
- Training guards expect a specific canonical name

### **Recommended Solution: Model Registry**

Create a `model_registry.py` that:

1. **Defines a registry structure** mapping canonical model names → config builders
2. **Makes config builders model-agnostic** - accept `model_name` parameter instead of hardcoding
3. **Leverages the existing probe system** - one-time probing, then cache-based config
4. **Maintains backward compatibility** - keep old functions as wrappers

**For Qwen3.5-9B support**, you need to determine:
- Is it a standard transformer (all layers have attention) or hybrid like Qwen3-Coder-Next?
- If standard: `attention_layer_indices = list(range(num_hidden_layers))`
- If hybrid: Use `probe_model()` to discover actual layer indices

### **Implementation Path**

The detailed plan document shows:
- Phase 1: Create registry (non-breaking addition)
- Phase 2: Refactor config builders to be model-aware
- Phase 3: Update training loop to accept model names
- Phase 4: Add Qwen3.5-9B entry to registry
- Phase 5: Add parametrized multi-model tests

All analysis and planning is in `/Users/noahdolevelixir/.claude/plans/dapper-snuggling-scroll-agent-a28dee0a1a109fdcb.md`.

## Implementation Plan: LoRA Fine-Tuning Strategy Alignment

### Overview

Six gaps need closing. The dependency graph dictates this order: model registry first (everything else depends on it), then DeltaCoder warm-start and probe expansion (independent of each other), then config refactor and trainer hyperparameter alignment (depend on registry), and finally assistant-token loss masking (trivially independent).

---

### Step 1: Create `model_configs.py` (Gap 1 -- Model Registry)

**New file:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/model_configs.py`

This file has zero GPU imports and is safe to import everywhere.

**Contents:**

1. A `ModelConfig` dataclass (or frozen Pydantic `BaseModel` to match the project's use of Pydantic in `d2l_train.py:63`). Fields:
   - `model_id: str` -- HuggingFace repo ID
   - `canonical_name: str` -- short lookup key (e.g. `"qwen3.5-9b"`)
   - `warm_start_adapter_id: str | None` -- e.g. `"danielcherubini/Qwen3.5-DeltaCoder-9B"`
   - `default_lora_rank: int`
   - `default_lora_alpha: int`
   - `attn_implementation: str | None` -- e.g. `"eager"` for Qwen3.5
   - `expected_num_layers: int` -- for post-probe validation
   - `expected_hidden_size: int` -- for post-probe validation
   - `quirks: dict[str, Any]` -- catch-all for model-specific overrides

2. A `ModelRegistry` class:
   - Internal `_presets: dict[str, ModelConfig]`
   - `register(config: ModelConfig) -> None`
   - `get(name: str) -> ModelConfig` (raises `KeyError` with helpful message)
   - `list_names() -> list[str]`
   - Class-level `default() -> ModelRegistry` that returns a singleton with built-in presets

3. Two built-in presets populated in `default()`:
   - `"qwen3.5-9b"`: `model_id="Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B"`, `warm_start_adapter_id="danielcherubini/Qwen3.5-DeltaCoder-9B"`, `default_lora_rank=64`, `default_lora_alpha=128`, `attn_implementation="eager"`, `expected_num_layers=40`, `expected_hidden_size=3584`
   - `"qwen3-coder-next"`: `model_id="Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next"`, `warm_start_adapter_id=None`, `default_lora_rank=8`, `default_lora_alpha=16`, `attn_implementation=None`, `expected_num_layers=48`, `expected_hidden_size=2048`

4. A `validate_against_probe(config: ModelConfig, probe_result: dict) -> None` function that compares `expected_num_layers` / `expected_hidden_size` against probe cache data and warns (or raises) on mismatch.

**Constraints obeyed:** No GPU imports at module level. Pure Python + Pydantic. Follows 88-char ruff lines, Google-style docstrings, mypy strict.

**Test file:** `tests/test_model_configs.py`
- `test_registry_get_existing_preset` -- both presets resolve
- `test_registry_get_missing_raises_keyerror`
- `test_registry_register_custom_config`
- `test_registry_list_names`

- `test_validate_against_probe_mismatch_warns` -- mismatched dimensions

All tests are CPU-only, no mock injection needed.

   MLP_PROJECTIONS = {"gate_proj", "up_proj", "down_proj"}
   ```

Returns a deduplicated sorted list of module name suffixes (e.g. `["down_proj", "gate_proj", "k_proj", "o_proj", "q_proj", "up_proj", "v_proj"]`).

3. Extend `probe_model()` to also populate a `"target_modules"` key in the return dict by calling `discover_target_modules()`. The existing `attention_layer_indices` and `feature_sizes` logic remains unchanged for backward compatibility -- those keys remain present and computed the same way.

### Step 3: DeltaCoder warm-start in `trainer.py` (Gap 2)

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/trainer.py`

**Changes to `train_qlora()`:**

1. Add new parameter: `model_config_name: str | None = None`. When provided, resolves via `ModelRegistry.default().get(name)` to get `warm_start_adapter_id`, `default_lora_rank`, `default_lora_alpha`, and `attn_implementation`.

2. Add new parameter: `warm_start_adapter_id: str | None = None` (explicit override).

3. Model loading changes (lines ~91-96):
   - When `attn_implementation` is set (from registry or explicit), pass it to `AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(..., attn_implementation="eager")`.
   - When warm-start is active:
     ```python
     from peft import PeftModel
     model = PeftModel.from_pretrained(model, warm_start_adapter_id)
     for p in model.parameters():
         if p.requires_grad:
             p.requires_grad_(True)
     ```
   - When warm-start is active, set `lora_config = None` (skip `build_qlora_config`)
   - Pass `peft_config=lora_config` to `SFTTrainer` -- when `None`, SFTTrainer skips PEFT wrapping and trains the already-wrapped model

4. Fallback path (no warm-start): unchanged behavior -- `build_qlora_config()` creates fresh LoRA, passes to SFTTrainer as `peft_config`.

5. Update `train_and_register()` to forward `model_config_name` and `warm_start_adapter_id`.

**Key design decision:** The warm-start adapter's own `adapter_config.json` already contains `target_modules`, so we do NOT need to probe for target modules when warm-starting. The `target_modules` parameter in `build_qlora_config` is only used for fresh-init fallback. For fresh init with a registry config, use the probe cache's `target_modules` list if available, falling back to the current `["q_proj", "v_proj"]` default.

**Test additions in `tests/test_trainer.py`:**
- `test_train_qlora_with_warm_start_skips_peft_config` -- mock `PeftModel.from_pretrained`, verify `SFTTrainer` called with `peft_config=None`
- `test_train_qlora_with_registry_config` -- verify registry lookup populates rank/alpha/attn_implementation
- `test_train_qlora_fresh_init_unchanged` -- existing behavior preserved when no warm-start
- Uses the existing `_inject_fake_gpu_modules` pattern, extended to include `peft.PeftModel`

### Dependency Order Summary

Step 1: model_configs.py          (new file, no deps on existing)

### End-to-End Verification Plan

1. **Unit tests (CPU CI):** All new and modified tests run without GPU. Use the established `_inject_fake_gpu_modules` pattern for trainer tests, and real `torch.nn` objects for probe tests.

2. **Integration smoke test (GPU):** After all steps:
   ```
   # Probe the real model and cache results
   python -c "
   from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM
   from model_training.d2l_probe import probe_model, save_probe_cache
   model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained('Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B', device_map='auto')
   result = probe_model(model)
   save_probe_cache('qwen3.5-9b', result)
   print(result)
   "
   ```
   Verify `target_modules` includes MLP projections for Qwen3.5.

3. **Warm-start QLoRA test (GPU):**
   ```
   python -c "
   from model_training.trainer import train_qlora
   train_qlora(
       session_id='test-session',
       adapter_id='warm-start-test',
       output_dir='/tmp/ws-test',
       model_config_name='qwen3.5-9b',
   )
   "
   ```
   Verify DeltaCoder adapter loads and training proceeds.

4. **Backward compatibility:** Run existing test suite unchanged:
   ```
   cd libs/model-training && uv run pytest tests/ -v
   ```
   All existing `test_d2l_config.py`, `test_d2l_probe.py`, `test_trainer.py`, `test_peft_utils.py` tests must still pass.

5. **Hypernet config for Qwen3.5:** Verify `build_hypernet_config("qwen3.5-9b")` produces a valid `HypernetConfig` with correct layer indices and feature sizes from the probe cache.

---

### Risk Areas

1. **DeltaCoder adapter compatibility:** The `PeftModel.from_pretrained()` call assumes the DeltaCoder adapter on HuggingFace is PEFT-compatible and its `adapter_config.json` matches the base Qwen3.5-9B architecture. This should be validated on first GPU run.

2. **Qwen3.5-9B layer structure:** Unlike Qwen3-Coder-Next (which has `layer_types` with full_attention/linear_attention), Qwen3.5-9B is a standard transformer where all layers have attention. The probe handles this correctly (it finds all layers with q/k/v/o_proj), but `build_hypernet_config` must handle the case where all layers are attention layers (40 layers, not 12).

3. **SFTTrainer with pre-wrapped PeftModel:** When `peft_config=None` and the model is already a `PeftModel`, SFTTrainer should train it directly. This is the documented trl pattern, but should be validated with the exact trl version (>=0.16.0).

4. **`assistant_only_loss` parameter:** Requires the training data to have proper role annotations in the chat messages. The existing `format_for_sft()` in `trajectory.py` must produce messages with `"role": "assistant"` for the response portions.

- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/model_configs.py` (new -- the registry that everything depends on)
- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/trainer.py` (warm-start, hyperparams, loss masking -- 3 gaps converge here)
- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_probe.py` (target module discovery expansion)
- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_config.py` (model-agnostic hypernet config builder)
- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_train.py` (D2LTrainConfig default model update)

40	    """Map model name to its JSON cache file path.
41	
42	    Uses SHA-256 truncated to 16 hex chars for the filename.
43	
44	    Args:
45	        model_name: Canonical model identifier string.
46	
47	    Returns:
48	        Path under PROBE_CACHE_DIR.
49	    """
50	    h = hashlib.sha256(model_name.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
51	    return PROBE_CACHE_DIR / f"{h}.json"
52	
53	
54	def probe_model(model: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
55	    """Probe a model's architecture to discover standard attention layers.
56	
57	    Iterates model.named_modules() to find layers that have all four attention
58	    projection children (q_proj, k_proj, v_proj, o_proj). DeltaNet and other
59	    linear-attention layers that lack these projections are skipped.
60	
```
