I now have a complete picture. Here are the bugs found:

---

## Issues Found in PR #23

### Bug 1 — `train_and_register` stores wrong rank in `AdapterRecord` when registry provides the default

**File:** `libs/model-training/src/model_training/trainer.py`, line 351

**Code:**
```python
rank=rank if rank is not None else 64,
```

**Problem:** When `model_config_name="qwen3.5-9b"` is passed and `rank=None`, `_resolve_training_params` correctly resolves `resolved_rank=64` from the registry (`mc.default_lora_rank`). But `train_qlora` trains with that resolved rank. However, when `train_and_register` builds the `AdapterRecord` to store in the database, it repeats the raw `rank` argument (still `None` from the caller) and falls back to the hardcoded `64` rather than reading `resolved_rank` back from params. This is a latent correctness issue today (both happen to be 64), but if a caller passes `model_config_name="qwen3-coder-next"` (whose `default_lora_rank=8`), `train_qlora` will train with rank 8, but `AdapterRecord` will record rank 64. The registry-resolved rank is computed inside `train_qlora` and never returned, so `train_and_register` can't see it.

**Historical context:** The `train_and_register` function was intentionally designed (commit `2e3a1e2`) so that the database record faithfully mirrors what was trained. The PR introduces a two-level resolution path (`_resolve_training_params` inside `train_qlora`) but `train_and_register` never receives the resolved value — it only sees the original `rank=None`.

**Fix:** `train_and_register` should also call `_resolve_training_params` (or expose `resolved_rank` from `train_qlora`) before building `AdapterRecord`.

---

### Bug 2 — LoRA alpha inconsistency between `build_hypernet_config` and `build_qlora_config`

**Files:** `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_config.py` (line 220), `libs/model-training/src/model_training/model_configs.py` (line 45/119)

**Code in `build_hypernet_config` (new generic path):**
```python
lora_alpha=rank * 2,
```

**Code in `ModelConfig` for `qwen3.5-9b`:**
```python
default_lora_alpha=32,  # for rank=64, this is rank/2, not rank*2
```

**Problem:** The `build_hypernet_config` generic path hardcodes `lora_alpha = rank * 2`, ignoring `mc.default_lora_alpha` from the registry entirely. For `qwen3.5-9b` with rank 64, the registry says alpha should be 32 (`rank/2`), but `build_hypernet_config` will produce alpha=128 (`rank*2`). This inconsistency means the hypernet is trained with a different alpha than what `train_qlora` uses when building fresh LoRA configs (which correctly reads `resolved_alpha` from the registry). The D2L hypernet and the QLoRA trainer will produce adapters with mismatched scaling.

**Historical context:** `build_qwen3_hypernet_config` also used `lora_alpha=lora_r * 2` (line 103), which was appropriate for Qwen3-Coder-Next where `default_lora_alpha=16` and `default_lora_rank=8` (ratio is indeed 2:1). But for Qwen3.5-9B, the strategy document set alpha=32 for rank=64 (ratio 0.5:1), so the `rank * 2` formula breaks.

**Fix:** `build_hypernet_config` should use `mc.default_lora_alpha` instead of `rank * 2`.

---

### Bug 3 — `discover_target_modules` double-iterates, producing false positives from nested modules

**File:** `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_probe.py`, lines 76–80

**Code:**
```python
for _name, module in model.named_modules():
    for child_name, child in module.named_children():
        short = child_name.split(".")[-1]
        if short in ALL_KNOWN_PROJECTIONS and isinstance(child, nn.Linear):
            found.add(short)
```

**Problem:** `model.named_modules()` already walks all modules in the tree (including every intermediate container). For each such module, the inner loop iterates its immediate children. This means every `Linear` projection gets visited multiple times — once for each ancestor module that has it as a (possibly indirect) child via `named_children()`. In practice `named_children()` only returns direct children, so duplicates are collected per-parent visit, but the outer `named_modules()` still produces redundant work. The more subtle issue is: if any `nn.Module` subcontainer happens to have a child whose name collides with `ALL_KNOWN_PROJECTIONS` (e.g., a wrapper class with an `out_proj` attribute wrapping non-attention logic), it will be incorrectly included in `target_modules`. This is especially relevant because `GDN_PROJECTIONS` includes `out_proj`, which is also a common name in standard attention layers — any standard attention layer's `out_proj` will now always be included in the GDN set even when GDN layers are absent.

**Historical context:** `probe_model` (added in commit `a78b9db`) carefully checks `ATTN_PROJECTIONS.issubset(child_names)` before recording a layer, avoiding false positives. The new `discover_target_modules` skips that guard, taking every `out_proj`-named Linear anywhere in the tree.

---

### Bug 4 — `_resolve_training_params` sets `attn_impl` unconditionally from registry even when `model_config_name` is set but `attn_implementation` is `None`

**File:** `libs/model-training/src/model_training/trainer.py`, line 72

**Code:**
```python
resolved["attn_impl"] = mc.attn_implementation
```

**Problem:** Unlike every other parameter in `_resolve_training_params`, `attn_impl` is always overwritten by the registry value — there is no explicit-override check like the other fields. This means a caller can't pass a different `attn_implementation` via an explicit argument because there is no such parameter in `train_qlora`'s signature anyway. But more importantly, if a future `ModelConfig` is registered with `attn_implementation=None`, calling `model_config_name` for it will *reset* `attn_impl` to `None` even if a non-None value was somehow in place. This is a design asymmetry — all other params respect the "explicit override wins" rule, but `attn_impl` does not. This is minor today (only two configs, one has `None`) but establishes a broken pattern.

---

### Summary Table

| # | File | Location | Severity | Type |

| 1 | `trainer.py` | `train_and_register`, line 351 | High — wrong rank in registry | Regression from registry integration |

| 4 | `trainer.py` | `_resolve_training_params`, line 72 | Low — no override path for attn_impl | Design asymmetry |

But the record construction uses:

)
```
`rank` here is the caller's raw argument, not `params["rank"]` (which would reflect the registry-resolved value). When `model_config_name="qwen3.5-9b"` is passed (registry default_lora_rank=64) with `rank=None`, the adapter trains at rank 64 (correctly resolved via `_resolve_training_params`), and the record stores 64 (correctly, by coincidence). But if a future model has `default_lora_rank=32` and the caller passes `rank=None`, the training runs at rank 32 while the stored record says rank 64. The docstring says it defaults to "registry value" but the record storage always uses the hardcoded fallback — the docstring's claim is inaccurate for the stored metadata.

Based on my analysis of PR #23's diff against all relevant past PR review comments, here are the applicable findings:

---

**Applicable issues from previous PRs:**

**1. Adapter file_path is a directory — warm-start path may be a HF Hub ID, not a local path (from PR #6, "Merge loads wrong file_path")**

PR #6 flagged that passing `file_path` (which can be a PEFT adapter *directory*) into load functions caused failures. PR #23 has a parallel issue: `warm_start_adapter_id` in the `qwen3.5-9b` preset is `"danielcherubini/Qwen3.5-DeltaCoder-9B"` — a HuggingFace Hub repo ID. In `trainer.py`, the call is `PeftModel.from_pretrained(model, str(warm_start))`. `PeftModel.from_pretrained` does accept Hub IDs, so this works on its face — but the code comment says "loads pre-trained adapter" without distinguishing local vs remote, and there is no auth/offline handling. If the training environment has `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1` set (common on GPU servers), this silently fails. The PR should document whether `warm_start_adapter_id` is expected to be a local path or a Hub ID, and add a guard or log message for the offline case. Source: PR #6 comment on `scripts/swarm_evolution.py:127`.

**2. `lora_config = None` branch is never consumed downstream (potential silent bug)**

In `trainer.py`'s warm-start path, `lora_config` is set to `None` and `build_qlora_config()` is skipped. However, the `SFTTrainer` call downstream receives `peft_config=lora_config` — passing `None` here means SFTTrainer runs without a PEFT config at all. For warm-start this is intentional (the model is already a `PeftModel`), but there is no assertion or type narrowing to confirm `model` is actually a `PeftModel` at the point of trainer construction. The `# type: ignore[assignment]` on `PeftModel.from_pretrained` suppresses the type mismatch. If `PeftModel.from_pretrained` raises (e.g. Hub 404, wrong adapter config), the `model` variable retains its pre-wrapped type and training silently proceeds on the base model with no LoRA config. A `try/except` with a clear `RuntimeError` would make this fail-fast. Source: PR #6 comment on `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_train.py` (empty span guard incomplete — same pattern of a guard path that doesn't cover an edge case).

**3. `_resolve_training_params` uses `# type: ignore[assignment]` on cast-from-dict (from PR #5/PR #3 typing conventions)**

The PR extracts typed values from an untyped `dict[str, object]` using multiple `# type: ignore[assignment]` casts:
```python
resolved_rank: int = params["rank"]  # type: ignore[assignment]
resolved_alpha: int = params["alpha"]  # type: ignore[assignment]
```
The project convention (enforced in PR #3/PR #5) is to use `X | None` union types and avoid `Optional[...]`. The same principle applies here: using `TypedDict` for `_resolve_training_params`'s return value would eliminate all five `type: ignore` suppressions and make the contract explicit. The current pattern was flagged implicitly in PR #3 (type annotation consistency) and PR #5 (docstring types not duplicated — but the obverse, not suppressing type errors with ignores, is equally important). Source: PR #3 comment on `libs/adapter-registry/src/adapter_registry/models.py`.

**4. Docstring missing `Raises:` on `_resolve_training_params` (from PR #6, PR #4)**

`_resolve_training_params` has `Args:` and `Returns:` but no `Raises:` section. It can raise `KeyError` when `model_config_name` is provided but not in the registry (via `ModelRegistry.get()`). PR #6 flagged exactly this pattern on `get_lineage_dag()` (Args + Returns but no Raises). The fix was adding a `Raises:` section. This is a `libs/` function (not `scripts/`), so the docstring exemption for scripts does not apply. Source: PR #6 comment on `libs/adapter-registry/src/adapter_registry/registry.py:297`.

**5. `ModelRegistry._default_instance` is a mutable class-level singleton — test isolation concern (from PR #3/PR #5 test patterns)**

`ModelRegistry.default()` caches a singleton in `_default_instance: ClassVar[ModelRegistry | None]`. Tests that call `registry.register(custom)` on a fresh `ModelRegistry()` are safe, but `test_registry_default_is_singleton` verifies the singleton property without resetting it between test runs. If a future test mutates the default registry (e.g. by registering a conflicting preset), it will affect all subsequent tests in the same process. The PR #3 pattern for this kind of shared state was to use `monkeypatch` to reset class-level state. No `monkeypatch` is used in the singleton test. This is low-severity but consistent with the "test isolation" feedback pattern seen across PRs #3 and #4.

---

**Issues from past PRs that do NOT apply:**

- PR #22's `pip install` / `uv` violation: PR #23 adds no shell instructions.
- PR #3's write-once adapter overwrite (`_save_sakana_adapter`, `exist_ok=True`): PR #23 doesn't touch `sakana_d2l.py`'s save path; the output directory for `train_qlora` is caller-controlled and already existed pre-PR.
- PR #5's boolean tensor naming (`keep_mask`, `tie_mask`): PR #23 introduces no boolean tensors.
- PR #3's nondeterministic `hash()` in hypernetwork: not touched by this PR.

Let me examine the issue more carefully by looking at what happens in `_resolve_training_params`:

The issue is **verified and real**:

**Line 46** in `_resolve_training_params`:
```python
"rank": rank if rank is not None else 64,
```
This runs *before* checking `model_config_name`. So if `rank=None` is passed, it defaults to 64 immediately.

**Lines 62-63** then override this *only if* `model_config_name` is set AND `rank is None`:
```python
if rank is None:
    resolved["rank"] = mc.default_lora_rank
```

This logic is correct—`_resolve_training_params` returns the properly resolved rank.

**However, the bug is in `train_and_register()` at line 351**:
```python
rank=rank if rank is not None else 64,
```

This line uses the *caller's raw `rank` argument*, not the resolved rank from `_resolve_training_params`. When `train_qlora()` is called at line 324, it *does* receive the raw `rank=None`, resolves it internally to 8 (from model registry), and trains at rank 8. But the `AdapterRecord` captures the unresolved `rank if rank is not None else 64`, recording rank=64 instead of the actual rank 8 used in training.

**Score: 75**

**Justification:**
- **Verified real:** The code at line 351 uses the caller's raw `rank` argument, bypassing the resolution logic that happened in `_resolve_training_params`.
- **Will hit in practice:** Anyone calling `train_and_register(..., model_config_name="qwen3-coder-next", rank=None)` will train at rank 8 but record rank 64 in the registry. This is a metadata mismatch that breaks adapter introspection and lineage tracking.
- **Not called out in CLAUDE.md:** The architecture documentation does not mention this metadata consistency requirement.
- **Not a nitpick:** Incorrect metadata in the AdapterRegistry violates the "lineage tracking" guarantee mentioned in the project description.

Fix: Pass the resolved rank from `_resolve_training_params` to the `AdapterRecord` constructor instead of the raw argument.

Looking at the code in `d2l_probe.py`, let me verify the issue claim:

**Claim 1: O(depth * modules) instead of O(modules)**

The problematic code is in `discover_target_modules()` (lines 76-80):
```python
for _name, module in model.named_modules():
    for child_name, child in module.named_children():
        short = child_name.split(".")[-1]
        if short in ALL_KNOWN_PROJECTIONS and isinstance(child, nn.Linear):
            found.add(short)
```

This nested loop structure is indeed problematic. For each module, it iterates over that module's direct children. Since `model.named_modules()` already traverses the entire tree, and we're calling `named_children()` on each module in that tree, we iterate over every parent-child relationship in the model. This is NOT O(depth * modules)—it's actually O(modules) because each module is visited once by the outer loop, and each child is visited once by the inner loop, and they collectively form a tree (each edge traversed once). However, we can do better: we should iterate over `model.named_modules()` once and extract the leaf name without nested iteration.

**Claim 2: `out_proj` in both `GDN_PROJECTIONS` and `ATTN_PROJECTIONS`**

Looking at lines 26-28:
```python
ATTN_PROJECTIONS = {"q_proj", "k_proj", "v_proj", "o_proj"}
GDN_PROJECTIONS = {"in_proj_qkv", "in_proj_z", "in_proj_b", "in_proj_a", "out_proj"}
MLP_PROJECTIONS = {"gate_proj", "up_proj", "down_proj"}
```

The issue mentions `out_proj` appears in both, but looking at the code, `out_proj` is in `GDN_PROJECTIONS` only, and `o_proj` (different name) is in `ATTN_PROJECTIONS`. So this part of the issue is **inaccurate** — there is no duplication between these sets based on the actual code.

**Score: 50**

**Justification:**

The nested loop inefficiency is real but minor—the code is still O(modules) overall because it's a tree traversal. A cleaner approach would directly iterate `model.named_modules()` and extract the short name without nested calls. The claim about `out_proj` duplication is **incorrect**; the code shows `o_proj` and `out_proj` are distinct keys in different sets. The issue correctly notes that discovering ALL projections is the intended behavior, which is accurate. The problem is correctly identified as something that works but could be more efficient/cleaner, making this a valid nitpick without critical impact.

Perfect. Now I have the full context. Let me analyze the issue:

**Key findings:**

1. **CLAUDE.md explicitly states** "mypy with strict-ish config" (line 31) as a convention
2. **The model-training library has `strict = true`** in its mypy config (the most restrictive setting)
3. The function `_resolve_training_params` returns `dict[str, object]` (line 27 of trainer.py)
4. The call site (lines 144-153) extracts 5 values, each requiring `# type: ignore[assignment]` (lines 155-159) to assign them to properly-typed variables
5. A `TypedDict` would eliminate all 5 suppressions and be fully compatible with strict mypy

**Assessment:**

This is a **real, verified issue**. The problem is:
- Direct contradiction to the stated convention (strict-ish mypy config)
- The `strict = true` setting in model-training's pyproject.toml means type ignores are actively discouraged
- A TypedDict is the standard solution for this exact pattern
- The fix is straightforward and improves code quality

However, this is not a high-frequency issue—it affects one function. The impact is localized. It's not a code smell that would recur everywhere, just a missed opportunity for type safety in one module.

**Score: 75**

**Justification:** Verified real issue that contradicts the project's explicit "mypy strict-ish" convention. The `strict = true` setting in model-training's config makes type ignores anti-patterns here. TypedDict is the obvious, standard solution. This improves type safety in a library that explicitly targets strict typing. Not a 100 because it's a single localized instance (not recurring pattern), but well above nitpick level given the explicit typing mandate in CLAUDE.md and pyproject.toml.

Perfect. Now I have enough information. Let me summarize my findings:

**Issue Analysis:**

1. **Line 220 of d2l_config.py** (in `build_hypernet_config` function):
   - Hardcodes `lora_alpha=rank * 2`
   - For rank=64, this produces alpha=128

2. **Registry default for qwen3.5-9b** (model_configs.py):
   - `default_lora_rank=64`
   - `default_lora_alpha=32` (which is rank/2)

3. **QLoRA trainer** (trainer.py, lines 62-65):
   - Uses `mc.default_lora_alpha` from registry when alpha is not explicitly provided
   - For qwen3.5-9b with rank=64, this resolves to alpha=32

4. **The mismatch:**
   - `build_hypernet_config()` produces a hypernetwork with lora_alpha=128
   - QLoRA trainer uses lora_alpha=32
   - Both are for the same model with the same rank, but with different scaling factors
   - This directly violates the principle stated in CLAUDE.md that "Three bugs fixed in Sakana D2L → PEFT conversion (combine_lora, alpha scaling, module paths)"

5. **No test catches this:**
   - test_build_hypernet_config_qwen35_with_probe_cache doesn't validate lora_alpha
   - No test compares hypernetwork alpha vs trainer alpha

This is a **real bug**: the hypernetwork adapter will have 4x the scaling amplitude of the QLoRA-trained adapter (128 vs 32), causing a mismatch when they're merged or compared.

**Score: 75**

**Justification:** This is a verified real issue that will definitely be hit in practice whenever hypernetwork and QLoRA training are used together on qwen3.5-9b (or any model with registry-specified alpha != rank*2). The scaling mismatch will cause incorrect adapter behavior during inference and merging. It's important for adapter consistency. The code plainly should use `mc.default_lora_alpha` like the trainer does, not hardcode `rank * 2`.

59	def discover_target_modules(model: Any) -> list[str]:
60	    """Discover all LoRA-targetable projection modules in a model.
61	
62	    Iterates model.named_modules() and collects the short names of any
63	    Linear children whose names match known projection patterns (attention,
64	    GDN, or MLP). Returns a sorted, deduplicated list.
65	
66	    Args:
67	        model: Any nn.Module (typically a transformer model).
68	
69	    Returns:
70	        Sorted list of unique projection names found (e.g.
71	        ["down_proj", "gate_proj", "k_proj", "o_proj", "q_proj", ...]).
72	    """
73	    import torch.nn as nn  # noqa: PLC0415
74	
75	    found: set[str] = set()
76	    for _name, module in model.named_modules():
77	        for child_name, child in module.named_children():
78	            short = child_name.split(".")[-1]
79	            if short in ALL_KNOWN_PROJECTIONS and isinstance(child, nn.Linear):
80	                found.add(short)
81	    return sorted(found)
82

93	    """Train a QLoRA adapter from a recorded coding trajectory.
94	
95	    Orchestrates the full training pipeline: load trajectory, format as SFT
96	    messages, build dataset, load model with NF4 quantization, train with SFT,
97	    and save the adapter to output_dir.
98	
99	    When model_config_name is provided, training defaults (rank, alpha, LR
100	    schedule, gradient accumulation, attention implementation) are loaded from
101	    the model registry. When the registry config specifies a warm_start_adapter_id,
102	    the pre-trained adapter is loaded via PeftModel.from_pretrained and training
103	    continues from those weights instead of initializing fresh LoRA matrices.
104	
105	    All GPU imports are deferred to this function body; the module is safe to
106	    import in CPU-only environments.
107	
108	    Args:
109	        session_id: Trajectory session ID to train from.
110	        adapter_id: Unique identifier for the resulting adapter.
111	        output_dir: Directory to save the trained adapter weights.
112	        base_model_id: HuggingFace model ID. Overrides registry default.
113	        task_type: Task category (e.g. 'code-gen', 'bug-fix').
114	        rank: LoRA rank. Defaults to registry value or 64.
115	        alpha: LoRA alpha scaling factor. Defaults to registry value or 128.
116	        epochs: Number of training epochs. Defaults to registry value or 3.
117	        learning_rate: Optimizer learning rate.
118	        model_config_name: Registry lookup key (e.g. "qwen3.5-9b"). When set,
119	            populates defaults from the model registry.
120	        warm_start_adapter_id: Pre-trained PEFT adapter to continue from.
121	            Overrides the registry's warm_start_adapter_id.
122	        gradient_accumulation_steps: Gradient accumulation steps. Defaults to
123	            registry value or 16.
124	        lr_scheduler_type: LR scheduler type. Defaults to registry value or
125	            "constant".
126	
127	    Returns:
128	        output_dir path where the adapter was saved.
129	
130	    Raises:
131	        FileNotFoundError: If the trajectory file does not exist.
132	        ValueError: If the trajectory is not successful or has no SFT messages.
133	    """
134	    # Deferred GPU imports — all in function body for CPU-only importability
135	    import torch
136	    from datasets import Dataset
137	    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer, BitsAndBytesConfig
138	    from trl import SFTConfig, SFTTrainer
139	
140	    from model_training.peft_utils import build_qlora_config
141	    from model_training.trajectory import format_for_sft, load_trajectory
142	
143	    # Resolve defaults from model registry and explicit overrides
144	    params = _resolve_training_params(
145	        model_config_name=model_config_name,
146	        base_model_id=base_model_id,
147	        warm_start_adapter_id=warm_start_adapter_id,
148	        rank=rank,
149	        alpha=alpha,
150	        epochs=epochs,
151	        gradient_accumulation_steps=gradient_accumulation_steps,
152	        lr_scheduler_type=lr_scheduler_type,
153	    )
154	    warm_start = params["warm_start"]
155	    resolved_rank: int = params["rank"]  # type: ignore[assignment]
156	    resolved_alpha: int = params["alpha"]  # type: ignore[assignment]
157	    resolved_epochs: int = params["epochs"]  # type: ignore[assignment]
158	    resolved_grad_accum: int = params["grad_accum"]  # type: ignore[assignment]
159	    resolved_lr_sched: str = params["lr_sched"]  # type: ignore[assignment]
160	    attn_impl = params["attn_impl"]

163	    resolved_model_id = params["base_model_id"]
164	    model_id: str = resolved_model_id or os.environ.get(  # type: ignore[assignment]
165	        "RUNE_BASE_MODEL", "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct"
166	    )
167

170	    messages = format_for_sft(trajectory)
171	    if not messages:
172	        raise ValueError(
173	            f"Trajectory {session_id} is not successful or has no SFT messages"
174	        )
175	
176	    # Build dataset
177	    dataset = Dataset.from_list([{"messages": messages}])
178	
179	    # NF4 quantization config (bfloat16 compute dtype prevents silent NaN loss)

181	        load_in_4bit=True,
182	        bnb_4bit_quant_type="nf4",
183	        bnb_4bit_compute_dtype=torch.bfloat16,
184	        bnb_4bit_use_double_quant=True,
185	    )

187	    # Load model and tokenizer

189	        "quantization_config": bnb_config,
190	        "device_map": "auto",
191	    }
192	    if attn_impl:
193	        model_kwargs["attn_implementation"] = attn_impl
194	
195	    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(model_id, **model_kwargs)
196	    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_id)
197	
198	    # Warm-start: load pre-trained adapter and enable gradient on LoRA params
199	    lora_config = None
200	    if warm_start:
201	        from peft import PeftModel
202	
203	        model = PeftModel.from_pretrained(model, str(warm_start))  # type: ignore[assignment]
204	        # Enable gradient on all adapter parameters for continued training
205	        for name, param in model.named_parameters():
206	            if "lora_" in name:
207	                param.requires_grad_(True)
208	    else:
209	        # Fresh LoRA initialization — try probe cache for target modules
210	        target_modules = ["q_proj", "v_proj"]
211	        if model_config_name:
212	            from model_training.d2l_probe import load_probe_cache
213	
214	            cache = load_probe_cache(model_config_name)
215	            if cache and "target_modules" in cache:
216	                target_modules = cache["target_modules"]
217	
218	        lora_config = build_qlora_config(
219	            rank=resolved_rank,
220	            alpha=resolved_alpha,
221	            target_modules=target_modules,
222	            dropout=0.1,
223	        )
224	
225	    # Training arguments
226	    training_args = SFTConfig(
227	        output_dir=output_dir,
228	        num_train_epochs=resolved_epochs,
229	        learning_rate=learning_rate,
230	        warmup_ratio=0.03,
231	        lr_scheduler_type=resolved_lr_sched,
232	        bf16=True,
233	        per_device_train_batch_size=1,
234	        gradient_accumulation_steps=resolved_grad_accum,
235	        save_strategy="no",
236	        logging_steps=1,
237	        report_to="none",
238	        eval_strategy="no",
239	        assistant_only_loss=True,
240	    )
241	
242	    # Create and run trainer
243	    trainer = SFTTrainer(
244	        model=model,
245	        args=training_args,
246	        train_dataset=dataset,
247	        peft_config=lora_config,
248	        processing_class=tokenizer,
249	    )
250	    trainer.train()
251	
252	    # Save adapter weights only (PEFT-aware: safetensors + adapter_config.json)
253	    trainer.save_model(output_dir)
254	
255	    return output_dir

280	        session_id: Trajectory session ID to train from.
281	        adapter_id: Unique identifier for the resulting adapter.
282	        base_model_id: HuggingFace model ID. Defaults to RUNE_BASE_MODEL env var.
283	        task_type: Task category (e.g. 'code-gen', 'bug-fix').
284	        rank: LoRA rank. Defaults to registry value or 64.
285	        alpha: LoRA alpha scaling factor. Defaults to registry value or 128.
286	        epochs: Number of training epochs. Defaults to registry value or 3.
287	        learning_rate: Optimizer learning rate.
288	        model_config_name: Registry lookup key (e.g. "qwen3.5-9b").
289	        warm_start_adapter_id: Pre-trained PEFT adapter to continue from.
290	        gradient_accumulation_steps: Gradient accumulation steps.
291	        lr_scheduler_type: LR scheduler type.
292	        database_url: SQLAlchemy database URL. Defaults to RUNE_DATABASE_URL env var
293	            or "sqlite:///{home}/.rune/rune.db".
294	
295	    Returns:
296	        adapter_id of the registered adapter.
297	
298	    Raises:
299	        FileNotFoundError: If the trajectory file does not exist.
300	        ValueError: If the trajectory is not successful or has no SFT messages.
301	    """
302	    from adapter_registry.models import AdapterRecord
303	    from adapter_registry.registry import AdapterRegistry
304	    from sqlalchemy import create_engine
305	
306	    # Resolve adapter output dir — read env var inside function body
307	    adapter_base = os.environ.get("RUNE_ADAPTER_DIR")
308	    if adapter_base:
309	        adapter_dir = Path(adapter_base) / adapter_id
310	    else:
311	        adapter_dir = Path.home() / ".rune" / "adapters" / adapter_id
312	    adapter_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
313	
314	    # Resolve model ID inside function body for testability
315	    model_id = base_model_id
316	    if model_id is None and model_config_name:
317	        from model_training.model_configs import ModelRegistry
318	
319	        model_id = ModelRegistry.default().get(model_config_name).model_id
320	    if model_id is None:
321	        model_id = os.environ.get("RUNE_BASE_MODEL", "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct")
322	
323	    output_dir = str(adapter_dir)
324	    train_qlora(
325	        session_id=session_id,
326	        adapter_id=adapter_id,
327	        output_dir=output_dir,
328	        base_model_id=model_id,
329	        task_type=task_type,
330	        rank=rank,
331	        alpha=alpha,
332	        epochs=epochs,
333	        learning_rate=learning_rate,
334	        model_config_name=model_config_name,
335	        warm_start_adapter_id=warm_start_adapter_id,
336	        gradient_accumulation_steps=gradient_accumulation_steps,
337	        lr_scheduler_type=lr_scheduler_type,
338	    )
339	
340	    # Compute file hash and size from the saved safetensors file
341	    adapter_file = adapter_dir / "adapter_model.safetensors"
342	    file_hash = hashlib.sha256(adapter_file.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
343	    file_size_bytes = adapter_file.stat().st_size
344	
345	    # Build the adapter record
346	    record = AdapterRecord(
347	        id=adapter_id,
348	        version=1,
349	        task_type=task_type,
350	        base_model_id=model_id,
351	        rank=rank if rank is not None else 64,
352	        created_at=datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc).isoformat(),
353	        file_path=output_dir,
354	        file_hash=file_hash,
355	        file_size_bytes=file_size_bytes,
356	        source="qlora",
357	        session_id=session_id,
358	    )
359	
360	    # Resolve DB URL inside function body for testability
361	    db_url = database_url or os.environ.get(
362	        "RUNE_DATABASE_URL",
363	        f"sqlite:///{Path.home() / '.rune' / 'rune.db'}",
364	    )
365	    engine = create_engine(db_url)
366	    registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
367	    registry.store(record)
368	
369	    return adapter_id

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "def discover_target_modules(model: Any) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Discover all LoRA-targetable projection modules in a model.\n\n    Iterates model.named_modules() and collects the short names of any\n    Linear children whose names match known projection patterns (attention,\n    GDN, or MLP). Returns a sorted, deduplicated list.\n\n    Args:\n        model: Any nn.Module (typically a transformer model).\n\n    Returns:\n        Sorted list of unique projection names found (e.g.\n        [\"down_proj\", \"gate_proj\", \"k_proj\", \"o_proj\", \"q_proj\", ...]).\n    \"\"\"\n    import torch.nn as nn  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n    found: set[str] = set()\n    for _name, module in model.named_modules():\n        for child_name, child in module.named_children():\n            short = child_name.split(\".\")[-1]\n            if short in ALL_KNOWN_PROJECTIONS and isinstance(child, nn.Linear):\n                found.add(short)\n    return sorted(found)",
  "new_string": "def discover_target_modules(model: Any) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Discover all LoRA-targetable projection modules in a model.\n\n    Single-pass iteration over model.named_modules() collecting the short\n    names of any Linear modules whose names match known projection patterns\n    (attention, GDN, or MLP). Returns a sorted, deduplicated list.\n\n    Args:\n        model: Any nn.Module (typically a transformer model).\n\n    Returns:\n        Sorted list of unique projection names found (e.g.\n        [\"down_proj\", \"gate_proj\", \"k_proj\", \"o_proj\", \"q_proj\", ...]).\n    \"\"\"\n    import torch.nn as nn  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n    found: set[str] = set()\n    for name, module in model.named_modules():\n        short = name.split(\".\")[-1]\n        if short in ALL_KNOWN_PROJECTIONS and isinstance(module, nn.Linear):\n            found.add(short)\n    return sorted(found)"
}

> AGENT

All 5 fixes pushed to PR #23:

1. **TypedDict for `_resolve_training_params`** — replaced `dict[str, object]` return with `_ResolvedParams(TypedDict)`, eliminating 5 `type: ignore` suppressions
2. **Rank mismatch in `train_and_register`** — now calls `_resolve_training_params` and uses `resolved["rank"]` for the `AdapterRecord` instead of `rank if rank is not None else 64`
3. **Alpha mismatch in `build_hypernet_config`** — uses `mc.default_lora_alpha` from the registry instead of hardcoded `rank * 2`
4. **Probe iteration efficiency** — `discover_target_modules` now uses a single `model.named_modules()` pass instead of nested `named_modules()` + `named_children()`
5. **Missing docstring attribute** — added `model_config_name` to `D2LTrainConfig` Attributes section

> AGENT

CI's mypy invocation passes fine. The error you saw is from running `uv run mypy` with a broader path that includes both `services/lora-server/config.py` and `scripts/eval/config.py`. Those two standalone `config.py` files without `__init__.py` parents collide as duplicate module names.

The simplest fix is to add `scripts/eval/` and `services/lora-server/` to the root mypy exclude pattern, since they're not package directories — they're standalone scripts.

> AGENT

PR created at https://github.com/ElixirTrials/rune/pull/24.

Those errors are all pre-existing `import-not-found` — they happen because `explicit_package_bases = true` changed how mypy resolves intra-package imports. The issue is that mypy can't find sibling modules within `libs/shared/src/shared/` etc. when using explicit bases.

Let me revert `explicit_package_bases` — it's not needed to fix the original duplicate module error. The exclude pattern alone is sufficient.

26	def test_apply_lora_adapter_calls_get_peft_model() -> None:
27	    """apply_lora_adapter delegates to peft.get_peft_model with (model, config).
28	
29	    Injects a fake 'peft' module into sys.modules so the deferred import inside
30	    apply_lora_adapter resolves without requiring the real peft package.
31	    """
32	    mock_model = MagicMock()
33	    mock_config = MagicMock()
34	    mock_peft_model = MagicMock()
35	    mock_get_peft_model = MagicMock(return_value=mock_peft_model)
36	
37	    # Build a fake peft module that has get_peft_model
38	    fake_peft = ModuleType("peft")
39	    fake_peft.get_peft_model = mock_get_peft_model  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
40	
41	    original = sys.modules.get("peft")
42	    sys.modules["peft"] = fake_peft
43	    try:
44	        result = apply_lora_adapter(mock_model, mock_config)
45	    finally:
46	        if original is None:
47	            del sys.modules["peft"]
48	        else:
49	            sys.modules["peft"] = original
50	
51	    mock_get_peft_model.assert_called_once_with(mock_model, mock_config)
52	    assert result is mock_peft_model

```

> AGENT

Three groups:

**6x `api-service` tests** (strict=True) — Endpoints for sessions and adapters are stubs that return 501 Not Implemented. The tests assert the 501 response and are marked xfail to document "we know these aren't built yet." These are intentional placeholders — they'll pass once the endpoints are implemented.

**4x `evolution-svc` tests** (strict=True) — Same pattern. Evaluate, evolve, promote, and prune adapter endpoints are stubs returning 501. Placeholder tests for future implementation.

**1x `test_train_and_register`** (strict=False) — The full GPU mocking chain (fake torch → fake transformers → fake SFTTrainer → fake AdapterRegistry) is fragile when test execution order varies. It sometimes fails depending on whether real GPU modules were loaded by other test files first.

The first 10 are deliberate "spec" tests for unimplemented endpoints — they're fine. The last one is the same test isolation issue we saw earlier with `setdefault` vs real modules. Want me to fix that one too using the `_force_inject_fake_gpu_modules` pattern?

> AGENT

This test mocks `train_qlora` entirely (line 104) and then checks that `train_and_register`:
1. Creates the adapter directory
2. Returns the adapter_id
3. Calls `registry.store()` once

It's testing glue logic — file path resolution and registry wiring. The GPU mocking chain (`_inject_fake_gpu_modules`) is only needed because `from model_training.trainer import train_and_register` triggers deferred imports at import time when the module hasn't been imported yet. But `train_qlora` is fully mocked out anyway, so the GPU chain adds zero test value — it just makes the import work.

The fix is simple: use `_force_inject_fake_gpu_modules(monkeypatch)` and drop the xfail.

> TOOL

Passes alone — fails in full suite because another test worker's `_force_inject_fake_gpu_modules` replaces the real `peft` module before this test runs. The xfail was masking the fact that this test needs real `peft` (which is installed locally but gets clobbered by parallel workers).

`test_build_qlora_config` calls `build_qlora_config` which does `from peft import LoraConfig` inside. When xdist workers clobber `peft` in `sys.modules`, this gets a MagicMock instead of real `LoraConfig`. The fix is to add a skip guard for when peft is a fake module.

For `test_train_and_register`, the issue is `patch("model_training.trainer.AdapterRegistry")` — since `AdapterRegistry` is imported inside the function body, it's not patchable as a module attribute. We need to patch the source module instead.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01EBafdSJgKfcqcfqRMxAbdL
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/tests/test_peft_utils.py",
  "old_string": "def test_build_qlora_config_returns_lora_config() -> None:\n    \"\"\"build_qlora_config returns a LoraConfig with the expected attributes.\"\"\"\n    result = build_qlora_config(rank=64, alpha=128, target_modules=[\"q_proj\", \"v_proj\"])",
  "new_string": "def test_build_qlora_config_returns_lora_config() -> None:\n    \"\"\"build_qlora_config returns a LoraConfig with the expected attributes.\"\"\"\n    peft_mod = sys.modules.get(\"peft\")\n    if peft_mod is not None and not hasattr(peft_mod, \"__file__\"):\n        pytest.skip(\"peft module is a fake injected by another test worker\")\n    result = build_qlora_config(rank=64, alpha=128, target_modules=[\"q_proj\", \"v_proj\"])"
}
```

  "new_string": "    with patch(\"model_training.trainer.train_qlora\") as mock_train:\n        mock_train.return_value = str(fake_adapter_dir)\n\n        # Patch at the source module level since train_and_register uses\n        # deferred imports (AdapterRegistry is not a module-level attribute)\n        mock_registry = MagicMock()\n        mock_registry_cls = MagicMock(return_value=mock_registry)\n        monkeypatch.setattr(\n            \"adapter_registry.registry.AdapterRegistry\", mock_registry_cls\n        )\n\n        from model_training.trainer import train_and_register\n\n        result = train_and_register(\n            session_id=session_id,\n            adapter_id=adapter_id,\n            database_url=\"sqlite:///:memory:\",\n        )\n\n    assert result == adapter_id\n    assert fake_adapter_dir.exists()\n    mock_registry.store.assert_called_once()"
}

> AGENT

Without xdist they pass/skip correctly. The issue is xdist worker isolation — when 12 workers run in parallel, one worker's `_force_inject_fake_gpu_modules` clobbers `sys.modules` for other workers since they share the same process namespace.

The real problem: `_force_inject_fake_gpu_modules` uses `monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules, ...)` which is scoped to the test but xdist workers can still interfere. These are flaky under xdist, not deterministic failures.

Let me run a few times to confirm:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_0145QHe38GSsbtjU3dJ9opyU
```json

  "command": "for i in 1 2 3; do echo \"--- Run $i ---\"; uv run pytest 2>&1 | tail -1; done",
  "description": "Run test suite 3 times to check flakiness"
}
```

--- Run 3 ---
=========== 1 failed, 421 passed, 10 xfailed, 56 warnings in 11.01s ============
```

> AGENT

Flaky — passes sometimes, fails sometimes. The root cause is `sys.modules` is shared across xdist workers in the same process. The proper fix is to isolate the trainer tests into their own xdist group so they don't interfere with tests that need real GPU modules.

> TOOL

> AGENT

Passes when run alone even with xdist. It only fails when the full suite runs. xdist workers ARE separate processes, so `sys.modules` shouldn't leak. The issue must be that `test_peft_utils.py` and `test_trainer.py` land in the **same worker** and the trainer tests' `_force_inject_fake_gpu_modules` replaces `peft` before `test_build_qlora_config` runs in the same worker.

The skip guard checks `hasattr(peft_mod, "__file__")` but the fake `ModuleType("peft")` also has `__file__` as `None`. Let me fix the guard:

> TOOL

> AGENT

`hasattr` returns False for a bare `ModuleType` — so the guard `not hasattr(peft_mod, "__file__")` should detect fakes. But wait, `monkeypatch.setitem` restores at teardown, so by the time `test_build_qlora_config` runs, `sys.modules["peft"]` should be restored... unless the tests run in the same worker and monkeypatch hasn't torn down yet.

Actually, xdist doesn't guarantee test ordering within a worker. If a trainer test with `_force_inject_fake_gpu_modules` runs first in the same worker, monkeypatch replaces `peft`, then `test_build_qlora_config` runs in that same worker while the monkeypatch is still active (same test session, different test function but monkeypatch is function-scoped so it tears down between tests).

Wait — monkeypatch IS function-scoped. It restores between each test. So by the time `test_build_qlora_config` runs, `peft` should be the real module again... unless the real `peft` import was cached with a different object.

Let me just check what's actually happening:

> AGENT

You're making the right point. Let me step back.

The GPU-mocked trainer tests verify things like:
- "does `train_qlora` raise `FileNotFoundError` for missing trajectories?" — this just tests `load_trajectory`, which has no GPU deps
- "does `SFTTrainer` get called with `peft_config=None` when warm-starting?" — verifies a MagicMock was called with None. Doesn't test that real PeftModel loading works.
- "does `SFTConfig` get `assistant_only_loss=True`?" — verifies a MagicMock received a kwarg

These tests mock away every GPU library and then check that the right MagicMocks were called with the right arguments. They don't test whether the code actually works on a GPU machine — they test the *wiring logic* between mocks. The value is marginal:

- The trajectory validation tests (missing/unsuccessful) are useful but don't need GPU mocks at all — they fail before reaching any GPU code
- The warm-start/registry/hyperparameter tests are essentially testing "did I pass the right kwargs to constructors" — a type checker does this better
- `test_build_qlora_config` tests that real `LoraConfig` has `.r` and `.lora_alpha` — that's testing peft's API, not our code

The only test with real value is `test_train_and_register_creates_adapter_dir` which verifies the file path wiring and registry store call. But even that mocks `train_qlora` entirely.

Want me to:
1. **Keep only the trajectory validation tests** (no GPU mocks needed) and delete the mock-heavy ones
2. **Keep all tests but remove the GPU mocking** — mark them as `requires_gpu` and skip in CI
3. **Leave as-is** and just fix the flakiness

137	    fake_datasets.Dataset = fake_dataset_cls  # type: ignore[attr-defined]

142	    fake_transformers.BitsAndBytesConfig = MagicMock()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
143	
144	    fake_trl = ModuleType("trl")

146	    fake_trl.SFTTrainer = MagicMock()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
147	
148	    fake_peft = ModuleType("peft")
149	    fake_peft.LoraConfig = MagicMock()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
150	    fake_peft.get_peft_model = MagicMock()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
151	    fake_peft.PeftModel = MagicMock()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
152	
153	    fake_bnb = ModuleType("bitsandbytes")
154	
155	    for name, mod in [

159	        ("trl", fake_trl),
160	        ("peft", fake_peft),
161	        ("bitsandbytes", fake_bnb),
162	    ]:

Here's my audit of every test:

| Test | What it actually tests | GPU mock needed? | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_train_qlora_function_importable_without_gpu` | Module imports without GPU | No | **Keep** — validates INFRA-05 |
| `test_train_qlora_rejects_missing_trajectory` | `load_trajectory` raises FileNotFoundError | No — fails before GPU code | **Keep, remove mock** |
| `test_train_qlora_rejects_unsuccessful_trajectory` | `format_for_sft` returns empty for exhausted | No — fails before GPU code | **Keep, remove mock** |
| `test_train_and_register_creates_adapter_dir` | File path wiring + registry.store call | Mocks `train_qlora` entirely | **Keep, remove GPU mock** — only needs train_qlora mock |
| `test_train_qlora_with_warm_start` | Asserts MagicMock called with None | No real value | **Delete** |
| `test_train_qlora_registry_config_sets_defaults` | Asserts MagicMocks received right kwargs | No real value | **Replace** with unit test of `_resolve_training_params` |
| `test_train_qlora_assistant_only_loss_enabled` | Asserts MagicMock received kwarg | No real value | **Replace** with unit test of `_resolve_training_params` |
| `test_train_qlora_fresh_init_backward_compat` | Asserts MagicMock peft_config not None | No real value | **Delete** |

The real tests are: importability, trajectory validation, `train_and_register` wiring, and `_resolve_training_params` logic. The mock-heavy ones should be replaced with direct tests of `_resolve_training_params` (pure Python, no GPU) and `requires_gpu`-marked integration tests.

  "content": "\"\"\"Tests for model_training.peft_utils module.\"\"\"\n\nimport sys\nfrom types import ModuleType\nfrom unittest.mock import MagicMock\n\nimport pytest\nfrom model_training.peft_utils import (\n    apply_lora_adapter,\n    build_qlora_config,\n    merge_adapter,\n)\n\n\ndef _gpu_available() -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Check if real peft is importable.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        import peft  # noqa: F401\n\n        return True\n    except ImportError:\n        return False\n\n\nrequires_gpu = pytest.mark.skipif(\n    not _gpu_available(),\n    reason=\"peft not available\",\n)\n\n\n@requires_gpu\ndef test_build_qlora_config_returns_lora_config() -> None:\n    \"\"\"build_qlora_config returns a LoraConfig with the expected attributes.\"\"\"\n    result = build_qlora_config(rank=64, alpha=128, target_modules=[\"q_proj\", \"v_proj\"])\n    assert result.r == 64\n    assert result.lora_alpha == 128\n    assert result.task_type == \"CAUSAL_LM\"\n    assert result.bias == \"none\"\n    assert \"embed_tokens\" not in result.target_modules\n    assert \"lm_head\" not in result.target_modules\n\n\ndef test_apply_lora_adapter_calls_get_peft_model() -> None:\n    \"\"\"apply_lora_adapter delegates to peft.get_peft_model with (model, config).\n\n    Injects a fake 'peft' module into sys.modules so the deferred import inside\n    apply_lora_adapter resolves without requiring the real peft package.\n    \"\"\"\n    mock_model = MagicMock()\n    mock_config = MagicMock()\n    mock_peft_model = MagicMock()\n    mock_get_peft_model = MagicMock(return_value=mock_peft_model)\n\n    # Build a fake peft module that has get_peft_model\n    fake_peft = ModuleType(\"peft\")\n    fake_peft.get_peft_model = mock_get_peft_model  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n\n    original = sys.modules.get(\"peft\")\n    sys.modules[\"peft\"] = fake_peft\n    try:\n        result = apply_lora_adapter(mock_model, mock_config)\n    finally:\n        if original is None:\n            del sys.modules[\"peft\"]\n        else:\n            sys.modules[\"peft\"] = original\n\n    mock_get_peft_model.assert_called_once_with(mock_model, mock_config)\n    assert result is mock_peft_model\n\n\ndef test_merge_adapter_raises_not_implemented() -> None:\n    \"\"\"merge_adapter raises NotImplementedError (out of scope for Phase 21).\"\"\"\n    with pytest.raises(NotImplementedError, match=\"merge_adapter\"):\n        merge_adapter(model=None)\n"

Clean. Here's what changed:

- **Deleted**: All `sys.modules` manipulation (`_inject_fake_gpu_modules`, `_force_inject_fake_gpu_modules`, `_remove_fake_gpu_modules`) — the entire fake GPU mock chain
- **Deleted**: 4 mock-heavy tests that only verified MagicMocks received the right kwargs (`test_train_qlora_with_warm_start`, `test_train_qlora_registry_config_sets_defaults`, `test_train_qlora_assistant_only_loss_enabled`, `test_train_qlora_fresh_init_backward_compat`)
- **Replaced with**: 5 pure Python tests of `_resolve_training_params` that test the actual parameter resolution logic — no mocks, no GPU, deterministic
- **Fixed**: Trajectory validation tests now call `load_trajectory`/`format_for_sft` directly instead of going through `train_qlora` with fake GPU deps
- **Added**: `requires_gpu` marker for future real GPU integration tests

> AGENT

These are TDD red-phase tests — they're supposed to fail until the endpoints are implemented. `xfail(strict=True)` is correct here: they fail now (xfail), and if someone implements the endpoint they'll xpass and force the marker to be removed. This is a valid pattern.

The fix is simple:
1. **Suppress the torch jit warning** via `filterwarnings`
2. **Add `requires_gpu` marker** registration so pytest doesn't warn about unknown markers
